# SQE — Next Steps

> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (cd): unquoted identifiers fold ASCII letters only (`fix/ident-fold-ascii`, #337).** DataFusion lowercases only the ASCII letters of an unquoted identifier, but DDL (`fold_unquoted_ident`) and the policy-predicate column stub used Unicode lowercase. `CREATE TABLE t (ÉTAT INT)` therefore stored `état`, and `SELECT ÉTAT` looked up `État` and missed. A row filter naming `ÉTAT` failed to parse the same way. Both now use `to_ascii_lowercase`. A property test uses DataFusion's own `IdentNormalizer` as the oracle for any quoted or unquoted name.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (cf): coordinator `memory_limit = "auto"` and the headroom check (`feat/coordinator-memory-limit-auto`, #808).** `coordinator.memory_limit = "auto"` sizes the pool from the enforced cgroup limit: limit x `memory_limit_auto_fraction` (default 0.7) minus the new `coordinator.process_headroom`. With no enforced limit it resolves to the old 8GB default. The resolved byte count is written back into the config before anything reads it, so the runtime, admission gate, write path and maintenance runtime all see one value. Like the worker, the coordinator now refuses to start when `memory_limit + process_headroom` exceeds the enforced limit, and it runs the runtime memory watch. Both roles share one check, `sqe_core::check_process_headroom`. An explicit size behaves as before. The Helm chart's coordinator (1500MB pool in a 2Gi pod) would fail that check with the 2GB default headroom, so the chart now renders `process_headroom = "512MB"` (2012MB < 2Gi). Worker values (6GB in 8Gi) pass unchanged.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-09 (ci): unindexed Hive tables read with their own `sqe.secret` again (`fix/hive-unindexed-store-registration`).** Since 2026-09-02 (d3938876, gzip detection), loading an unindexed Hive table built the store for its `sqe.secret` but never registered it on the session runtime. The compression block replaced the `register_object_store` line. `ListingTable` resolves its store from that registry at scan time, so these scans read with whatever the registry held for the bucket, normally the engine's own credentials. Affected: every unpartitioned table, and partitioned tables with neither a partition index nor projection. The path check in `store_for` and the one-secret-per-bucket claim kept running; only the credential that read the data was wrong. Where the engine's credentials could not reach the bucket, the table read as zero rows without an error. The registration is restored after the bucket claim. `an_unindexed_table_reads_through_its_secrets_store` (hive_provider_test) gives the secret's store a filesystem view the default store lacks, and returned 0 rows instead of 1 before the fix.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (ch): Hive scans plan without reading data files (`fix/hive-plan-time-footer-reads`, #807).** DataFusion 55 removed the per-table `with_collect_stat` opt-out, so with the session default `collect_statistics = true` every `ListingTable::scan` behind a Hive table read each listed file's footer while planning: 1,000 GETs before the first row of a 1,000-file table. Every Hive scan now plans on a copy of the session with the knob off (`without_footer_statistics`; indexed, lazy-limit and unindexed paths); the `read_parquet` family keeps the session as it is. Nothing used those reads: `HiveTableProvider::statistics` answers the logical planner from the manifest, and the scan's physical `num_rows` was `Absent` with the reads as well as without. Local, 1,000 one-row files: planning went from 252 ms with 1,000 data-file reads to 33 ms with 0. `crates/sqe-catalog/tests/it/hive_plan_io_test.rs` counts data-file reads on the session's store while planning and failed before the fix (10 and 4 reads). Left out: the ETag-keyed schema cache from the issue.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (cc): `ON` keys spanning cross-joined relations are kept (`fix/join-key-spanning-cross-join`, #806, apache/datafusion#26058).** DataFusion 55.1's `eliminate_cross_join` pools every inner join's `on` pairs and rebuilds the tree greedily. It drops a pair it cannot place: one whose side reads two relations that were joined without an equi-key, such as `a CROSS JOIN k JOIN b ON b.t = a.t AND b.id = a.x + k.k` (42 rows instead of 2). Placement depends on the greedy leaf order, so even keys over equi-joined relations can be dropped (`b JOIN (a JOIN k ON a.id = k.id) ON b.t = a.t AND b.id = a.x + k.k`). Keys written in `WHERE` were never affected. `engine_context` (coordinator, `sqe-cli` embedded, Hive view planning) now swaps the rule for `sqe_catalog::eliminate_cross_join`: a copy of the 55.1 source that puts every unplaced `on` pair back on top as a filter, next to the join filters. A NULL-safe join gets `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`. That is the fix proposed for upstream. A plan with no unplaced pair is exactly what upstream builds; a test pins this for placed keys, including one that spans two equi-joined relations. A pre-rule that moved every multi-leaf key into the join filter was tried and dropped: it also moved keys DataFusion would have placed, and the next pass's `extract_equijoin_predicate` fought it. The affected shape now runs as a nested-loop join over the same cross product the buggy plan built, with the missing filter applied. The tripwire `plain_datafusion_still_drops_the_key` fails once DataFusion fixes #26058; then delete the copy. Upstream PR not filed.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (cg): quick-xml advisories cleared without the stack upgrade (`fix/quick-xml-rustsec-0194-0195`, #789).** RUSTSEC-2026-0194/0195 (quick-xml < 0.41, due 10-25) came in only through object_store 0.13.2's `^0.39`. The object_store 0.14 / arrow 60 upgrade (!1133) cannot land before DataFusion 56 ships, so `vendor/object_store` is 0.13.2 as published with quick-xml raised to `^0.41` (the same change 0.14 made over the same call sites), wired in through `[patch.crates-io]`. quick-xml 0.39.4 is gone from Cargo.lock, both exceptions are removed from `deny.toml` and `.cargo/audit.toml`, and `cargo deny check advisories` and `cargo audit` pass. object_store's 184 unit tests (S3/Azure/GCS XML fixtures included) pass on 0.41, and `make test-integration` passes 241/0 (including the Hive-on-object-storage and `read_parquet`/`read_csv` tests). **Drop the patch** when DataFusion moves to object_store 0.14 (!1133 + !1107).
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-08 (ce): property tests against existing oracles (`test/property-oracles`, #639 item 4).**
> - **Trino types.** Every scalar Arrow type's Trino name parses back through `parse_trino_type` to a non-opaque type, with decimal precision/scale and timestamp precision/zone intact. Its `typeSignature` keeps parameters out of `rawType` (#345).
> - **Error classifiers.** `has_status_token` is checked against a tokenizer oracle. A 401 marker classifies as AuthenticationFailed under any surrounding noise, and a port containing `401` stays a timeout. Expired engine credentials win over `403 Forbidden`. Both classifiers ignore ASCII case.
> - **OCSF mapping.** Caller text (user, groups, client label, query text, error message) lands verbatim through a serialize/parse round trip, and a missing value is an absent key.
>
> **Open:** nested types (`array(...)`, `map(...)`, `row(...)`) still emit a parameterized `rawType` with no arguments; tracked in its own issue.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-07 (cb): recursive CTEs keep every iteration's MIN/MAX (`fix/recursive-cte-aggregate-dynamic-filter`, apache/datafusion#26054).** DataFusion's `RecursiveQueryExec` resets the recursive term between iterations, but `AggregateExec` keeps the MIN/MAX dynamic filter the first iteration pushed into its scan, so later iterations scanned with a stale bound and returned NULL or wrong rows (SQE's defaults enable the pushdown). User contexts (coordinator, `sqe-cli` embedded, Hive view planning) are now built by `sqe_catalog::engine_context`, whose `EngineQueryPlanner` plans a plan holding `WITH RECURSIVE` without `enable_aggregate_dynamic_filter_pushdown`; every other plan is unchanged. The tripwire test `plain_datafusion_still_loses_later_iterations` fails once DataFusion fixes #26054; then drop the planner. Next from the same report: an SQE issue for apache/datafusion#26058 (`eliminate_cross_join` drops an equi-key spanning two cross-joined relations; wrong rows on 55.1), then probes on hash-join build memory accounting, one-row cross joins and deep CTE filter-chain planning time.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-07 (ca): EXISTS and IN (subquery) no longer switch the row-filter guard on (`fix/leakproof-subqueries`, #805).** Both only answer true/false/NULL for the outer row, so `leakproof.rs` now counts them as leakproof; `IN (subquery)` still counts as fallible when the outer and subquery types do not meet in a lossless widening. Scalar subqueries stay fallible ("more than one row" depends on the data). Subquery bodies are still walked as plans of their own (`apply_with_subqueries`), so a fallible predicate inside one keeps the guard on (`fallible_predicate_inside_a_subquery_is_guarded`). **Guard cost, TPC-H SF1 A/B** (`tests/it/guard_ab.rs`, `#[ignore]`, local Parquet, plain plan vs a trivially-true row filter on `lineitem`, median of 11 interleaved runs): only q08, q12 and q14 trip the guard (most TPC-H arithmetic sits in aggregate arguments, which run above the filter), and they land at 0.95/0.99/1.00x plain; the suite total is 0.99x, inside run-to-run noise. Pruning loss on a partitioned Iceberg table is not covered by this rig.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-07 (bz): writes read the target through the row-filter guard (`fix/dml-row-filter-error-oracle`, #805).** DELETE and UPDATE evaluate the user's WHERE / SET over a policy relation that projects every column as `CASE WHEN <row filter> THEN <mask or col> END`, plus the row filter's TRUE-only verdict on the stored values (`__pol_admit_<nonce>`) and, for UPDATE, a raw alias per column so unchanged and hidden rows are written back as stored. A row filter alone stays inline; masks still go through the mask-context view. MERGE's target relation does the same, and `raw_target_col` returns the raw alias for every column under a row filter. The DELETE / match evaluators coerce a NULL verdict to FALSE: a row whose WHERE or row filter is NULL is no longer deleted by copy-on-write DELETE. User SQL naming a `__pol_` alias is refused. Always on for writes: the scratch table has no scan pruning to lose. **Open:** remaining items tracked in #805.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-07 (by): row-filter error oracle closed, scan pruning kept (`fix/row-filter-error-oracle`, carries !1316, #805).** The optimizer merges a caller's WHERE with the policy row filter into one conjunction, and AND evaluates its right side over the whole batch unless few rows pass, so `WHERE 1 / (id - 2) = 1` could raise an error from a hidden row. !1316 read every row-filtered column through `CASE WHEN <row filter> THEN col END`, which cost all pruning. Now `sqe-policy/src/leakproof.rs` classifies caller expressions the optimizer can move below a row filter (filters, join conditions, scan filters, projection / group-by / window-partition expressions). Comparisons, `IN`, `BETWEEN`, constant-pattern `LIKE`, boolean logic and lossless casts are leakproof. Arithmetic, functions, subqueries and casts that can fail are not, and neither is any comparison whose coercion casts a column lossily. The guard is only added when the plan holds a fallible expression (a plan-wide switch, because aliases hide which scan column a predicate reads). A fallible predicate already pushed into a scan (a view body) is lifted above the guard. `write_predicates` recognises the guard by its shape, so a mask directly over a row filter is still extracted. **Open:** remaining items tracked in #805, including an SF1 A/B of guarded queries.
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-07 (bx): catalog-vended, table-scoped S3 credentials (`feat/polaris-vended-credentials`).** SQE now sends `X-Iceberg-Access-Delegation: vended-credentials` when `[catalog] access_delegation = "vended-credentials"` or `require_vended_credentials` is set. The vendored REST client lets the table's vended keys beat the static `[storage]` pair, picking the `storage-credentials` entry with the longest matching prefix and falling back to `config` (vendor patch, marked `SQE PATCH (vended-credentials)`). The table metadata cache, stored prepared plans and distributed fragments all honour `s3.session-token-expires-at-ms`. The refresh-task stub is gone: it reloads the table as the querying user and pushes the new set to workers. `distributed_credentials = "vended"` ships the table's set, including the token, and never the engine key. Verified against the local Polaris 1.7 stack with static keys blanked (`vended_only_ctas_insert_select_roundtrip`, `needs:polaris`). **NEXT:** an e2e that lets a token expire mid-scan on the two-worker rig; an STS-backed quickstart (Polaris `stsEndpoint` to an emulator) with no static key; and calling the catalog's `client.refresh-credentials-endpoint` instead of a full reload. File TVFs, Hive and distributed compaction still use the static pair (documented in `s3vending.md`).
>
> **STATUS 2026-10-05 (bw): Trino-compat cleanup after DataFusion 55; SF100 data path, steps 1-2 (!1262-!1266).** !1262 the empty-input ROLLUP wrap is gone: DataFusion 55 emits the grand-total row itself (`init_empty_grouping_sets`), so ROLLUP/CUBE/GROUPING SETS reach it as written; results unchanged except `GROUPING SETS ((a),(b))` on empty input, which no longer gets an invented all-NULL row (Trino returns none). TPC-DS q67 SF10 saves 2-3 s CPU summed over cores, wall time unchanged. !1263 two more compat rewrites retired (recursive-CTE column lists, bare `ROW(...)`), pinned by `native_trino_syntax` probes; an unaliased `ROW(...)` column is now named `row(...)` over Flight. !1264 `from_unixtime` returned NULL for a DECIMAL argument (`parse_float_as_decimal`); signature now coerces to Float64. q67 closed as not-SQE: what remains is DataFusion expanding every input row into all 9 ROLLUP levels (4.8M rows -> ~43M hash inserts; a level-from-level cascade would be ~11.4M, ~1 s); an upstream issue is drafted, not filed. SF100 data path (#783, #416): the generator streaming #783 asked for was already merged (!1222); peak RSS SSB 84 MB SF1 / 110 MB SF10, TPC-H bound by threads (89 MB at 1, 643 MB at 11), TPC-C 538 MB and ClickBench 912 MB at SF10 (still materialize). !1265 one layout definition (partition, sort-on-write, blooms) for `load` and the direct Iceberg sink; the sink refuses tables it would lay out differently, adds the engine's `_sk` auto-blooms, and the SSB `dim_date` bloom key (was `ddate`) is fixed. !1266 the direct sink writes TPC-H/TPC-C partitioned like `load` through one bounded buffer per table (`--partition-buffer`, 2g): TPC-H SF10 lineitem 269 files / 2.5 GB (load 415 / 2.6 GB), 3.5 GB peak, 39 s suite. Sizing for the SF100 host: ~210 GB of warehouse data, 1 TB NVMe recommended, 64 GB+ RAM. NEXT: date-ordered fact generation for SSB/TPC-DS/TPCBB (changes generated values, so a fresh SF1/SF10 baseline), then stream TPC-C/ClickBench and switch the bench script to the direct sink; SF100 runs wait for the host.

> **STATUS 2026-10-03 (bv): platform-integration batch merged, one MR per item (!1246-!1254).** !1246 https `nextUri` behind a TLS ingress: `[coordinator] public_base_url`, CIDR ranges in `[security] trusted_proxies`, a trusted `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` never downgrades. !1248 the per-token catalog circuit breaker counts upstream faults only (`[catalog] circuit_breaker_failure_classes`, default 5xx/timeout/connection), logging subject and reason. !1249 `SHOW SCHEMAS FROM <catalog> [LIKE|ILIKE]`; an unknown catalog is `CatalogNotFound`, as in Trino (no `ESCAPE`: sqlparser lacks it). !1250 a namespace miss inside the 5 s re-list cooldown probes the catalog once, and every write invalidates the namespace listing for all tokens (dbt `CREATE SCHEMA` on another replica). !1251 file-TVF preparation is per statement (task-local, keyed by function and args): two concurrent statements on one token no longer race "not prepared before planning"; every production `ctx.sql` goes through `plan_sql`, enforced by `planner_entry_lint`. !1252 `make test-integration` runs on bash 3.2. !1253 optional TLS Trino listener: `trino_https_port` + `[coordinator.trino_tls]`, hot-reloaded every 60 s (cert-manager), https `nextUri` on that port, TLS-only with `trino_http_port = 0`. !1254 `[catalogs.<name>] tvf_prefixes` add to the global TVF allowlist only in sessions scoped to that catalog, and only when its namespace listing answered the caller's own bearer (`SqeCatalogProvider::listing_succeeded`) under per-user REST auth: the session catalog is client-chosen, so the name alone opens nothing. Integration on !1254: `test_read_` 3/3, `hive_external` 3/3; full suite not re-run (12 pre-existing failures on main: distributed_failure_e2e x5, maintenance_procedures x5, test_error_classification_live, mcp_listener_supervision). > **STATUS 2026-10-02 (bu): SF10 follow-ups merged; scan statistics report the projected size (`fix/scan-stats-projected-bytes`).** Merged from (bt): !1238 runtime-filter evaluation, !1239 TPC-DS sizing + stale part files, !1240 scan admission scaled to the projected columns (#801: TPC-DS q09/q14/q28 run at 4 GiB), !1241 TPCBB q05/q08/q10 pre-aggregate per channel (#802: 30/36/22 s -> 2.6/2.4/3.3 s, old sums were inflated ~4,000x), !1242 commit reload retries a transient 401/5xx (#803 root cause open). New: `IcebergScanExec` statistics reported `total_byte_size` as whole-file compressed bytes regardless of projection, and `JoinSelection` compares bytes first, so TPC-DS q72 built its hash table on inventory (133M rows, narrow) against catalog_sales (14M rows, wide) cut to 3.7K by runtime filters: 19.6 s of build CPU. Now rows x width for fixed-width projected columns, manifest compressed size x 4 for variable-width. Plans change on 29 of 125 explained TPC-H/SSB/TPC-DS SF10 queries (no SSB); paired A/B over those 29: 44.1 s -> 38.7 s, q72 4.23 -> 1.70 s, q50 1.42x, tpch q13 1.31x, no row-count changes. Closed as not-engine: TPC-DS q10's 5.8 s (0.6-1.7 s alone; suite run was under Defender load). Open: q67 (8-column ROLLUP + rank over 6.5M rows). Corrected: the 18 GiB load spill was the ~1.17B rows the stale part files added; correctly sized SF10 inventory (133M) sorts on write with zero spill in 15.6 s (#788 note).

> **STATUS 2026-10-02 (bt): SF10 slow-query pass over every suite (`perf/sf10-dynamic-filter-eval`, sibling `fix/tpcds-generator-sizing`).** On the Mac rig TPC-H and SSB SF10 are store-bound: every slow query reads at 160-230 MB/s, and plain 32-way GETs from RustFS top out at 170 MB/s, so wall time is bytes / ~200 MB/s and only fewer bytes moves it (q21 reads lineitem 3x, q18 2x). CPU fixes in the scan's runtime-filter path, all in `sqe-catalog`: a sealed partitioned-join filter (`CASE hash % n WHEN p THEN keys IN set_p`) is collapsed once per scan into one `IN` over the union (45% of q21's CPU was the per-arm CASE); `wait_for_dynamic_filters` reads the filter generation instead of rebuilding a 700K-literal tree (13%); `convert_case` bails on a lower bound before building an over-cap set (12%); a join-membership filter that keeps >=95% of a 1M-row sample is skipped and re-probed every 16th batch (bank q06: 15 s CPU over 140M rows removing nothing). q21 CPU -30%, wall unchanged on this rig. Sibling: TPC-DS item/warehouse followed linear scaling above sf1, so inventory was 1.17B rows at sf10 (official 133M) and its sorted load spilled 18 GiB; and `generate` never cleared a table directory, so a regeneration with fewer part files loaded the old ones too. SF10, dev-release, 8 GiB pool / 4 GiB per-query cap: TPC-H 22/22 47-50 s, SSB 13/13 30 s, TPC-C 17/17, TPC-E 18/18, ClickBench 43/43 (1M rows at "SF10"), bank 8/8 36 s, TPC-DS 96/99 90 s, TPCBB 10/10 190 s. Open: scan decode admission reserves 4x the whole split, not the projected columns (TPC-DS q09/q14/q28 refused at 4 GiB within 80 ms); TPCBB q05/q08/q10 chain three fact LEFT JOINs on one customer key (fan-out; the SUMs are inflated, Trino agrees with the wrong answer); one CTAS commit got a 401 that Polaris never logged; TPC-DS q10/q72/q67 are 3-5 s over the I/O floor, unprofiled.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-29 (bs): session scope reaches policy and catalog resolution (`fix/use-scope-policy`).** Security: a table named without its schema under a session default schema (`USE sales` over Flight, `X-Trino-Schema`, the MCP default) was governed as `default.<t>`, because DataFusion keeps the scan's reference as written and `resolve_policy_key` keyed a bare name in `default`: a row filter on `sales.t` was skipped for `SELECT * FROM t` (rewriter-level repro, 2 vs 3 rows). `PolicyEnforcer::evaluate` now takes a `TableScope` read from the planning `SessionContext`; the rewriter qualifies each scan reference before keying (a reserved catalog is never filled in, so no user table turns "virtual"). Also `USE <hive-root>.<db>` fell back to the config default catalog, so unqualified names read the same-named Iceberg table: the benchmark's Hive mode has only ever measured Iceberg (the 09-27 `*-hive-*` reports are Iceberg numbers). Tests: `default_scope_policy` (3), `an_unqualified_name_under_a_hive_default_catalog_reads_the_hive_table`; both end-to-end ones fail with the fix reverted. Sibling `fix/hive-unpartitioned-empty`: the post-write index refresh persists an empty partition index for an unpartitioned table and the provider planned from it, so every unpartitioned Hive CTAS/INSERT table read as 0 rows (the reader now ignores a partition index on a table with no partition keys). With both, the first real Hive TPC-H SF1: 22/22, rows identical to Iceberg, 5.4s vs Iceberg 8.5s (1.57x; 5 large files vs 84 monthly partitions). Found on the way, open: `GRANT ... ROWS WHERE` does not parse over Flight SQL.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-29 (br): Parquet read amplification (`perf/parquet-read-amplification`).** The recurring TPC-H q21 gap was bytes, not joins: the vendored reader's 512 KiB footer prefetch and 1 MiB range-coalesce gap over-read small files 2.4x (q21 SF1: 408 MB read, 152 MB projected; a per-file model from the Parquet offsets reproduces `bytes_scanned` to 0.1 MB). Both defaults now 64 KiB (`vendor/iceberg-rust/crates/iceberg/src/arrow/reader/mod.rs`, patch table updated). Same-binary A/B: q21 SF1 2.95s -> 1.30s, no coalescing at all is slower (1.7s). Bench runs: TPC-H SF1 14.9s -> 8.3s (2.6x Trino, 22/22, q21 1.06s vs Trino 1.64s), SF10 66.1s -> 46.1s, q02 +105 ms the only >5% slower. Open: validate the 64 KiB gap on StorageGRID / real S3 (higher per-request latency); Hive tables read through DataFusion's own ParquetSource, which keeps its defaults. Separately `fix/bench-compare-qualify`: compare scoped the SQE client with no schema and the qualifier missed `BETWEEN (SELECT ...)`, failing tpcds q54 and tpcc stock_level in compare only. Known generator gap: tpce SF1 `holding`/`holding_summary` have 35 distinct (ca_id, symb) keys, trade_result fans out to 22M rows.

Closed as obsolete: #406 #383 (shuffle tree deleted), #698 (all children done). Security audit #708 fully closed once #716 merges.

> **STATUS 2026-09-21 (bo): dbt compatibility batch (#767-#773) and #700 guard.** Stock dbt-trino and the ADBC clients broke on four wire and DDL gaps found 2026-09-21: #771 a no-result statement through Flight SQL ExecuteQuery now yields a schema frame then end-of-stream (was EOF); #767 `POST /v1/statement` never inlines data, Go clients page via `nextUri`; #768 `CREATE VIEW ... SECURITY DEFINER` parses on every door (shared front-door fallback); #769 `system.metadata.materialized_views` exists (empty, Trino columns; `table_comments` already did); #770 `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` references schema-id -1 so Polaris resolves the just-added schema; #773 streaming CTAS narrows ns timestamps to µs on v1/v2 tables; #772 dbt-sqe is pip-installable (package data, no shadowing `__init__.py`, DDL via ExecuteUpdate). #700 FULL OUTER JOIN over a window CTE did NOT reproduce in seven shapes through the full coordinator path (guard test `full_outer_window_join_test`); likely a post-join filter on the window side. Security audit #708: 35/38 closed, #716 open.

> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bn): security audit #708 batch, 27 of 30 children fixed in six themed MRs.** Validated at source, one test each. Critical #709: Trino OAuth2 pending session keyed by sha256(auth_id), poll one-shot, initiate never mints. auth/web: #710 `aud` required when configured, #726 forwarded headers only from trusted_proxies, #712 GetTables quotes identifiers. grants/DDL: #721 SHOW GRANTS self-or-admin, #720 deny-lift needs Ranger-authorized POST or admin (`RevokeStatement.grantor_is_admin`), #747 SET/UNSET TAGS admin, #725 `system.runtime.queries` redacted. hive: #715 metadata policy keyed `{root}.{db}` like SELECT, #717 CREATE EXTERNAL/DROP gated, #711 EXPLAIN accepts only a query. write: #731 DML IN-subqueries run through the enforcer, #729 INSERT/DoPut refuse rows outside the caller's row filter (Hive append open). engine boundary: #719 production_mode secret rule ignores worker_urls, #724 audience fails closed (static workers need `worker.advertise_url` = dial URL), #730 compaction requests carry audience+expiry, #727/#728 GCS inline base_url and key path gated, #723/#749 change tool and `quack_query` denylisted, #704(b) allow-all-buckets + anonymous MCP must be loopback, #718 engine-credential catalogs need explicit `write_catalogs`. CI/vendor: #713 no `--clobber`, #714 moot (ci_separated_caches=true) + machete pinned, #748 vendored REST client keeps the bearer when `/v1/config` advertises SigV4. NOT-A-BUG #722 (health_check is liveness, not trust). Open: #716 (info_schema Hive listing, same class as #664/#665), #729 Hive append, #673 #674 feature work.

> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bm): MCP `execute_sql_readonly` is catalog-query only (#703 #704).** File TVFs (`read_parquet`, `read_csv`, `read_json`) and query-shaped writes (`SELECT INTO`, `WITH … INSERT`) are refused at the classifier gate: they were `StatementKind::Query` and skipped Ranger/catalog. Tool description, server instructions and the MCP docs say so. Test `read_only_guard_rejects_file_tvfs_and_query_shaped_writes`. Flight SQL / Trino still have the TVFs.

> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (be): `execute_sql_readonly` tells agents not to scan `information_schema` catalog-wide (`feat/mcp-info-schema-guidance`); lazy per-schema build is #701; Hive tag masks (cycle 3) pointer is #702.**

> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (bd): `traceparent` on the MCP path (`feat/mcp-traceparent`).** Flight SQL and Trino HTTP adopted the caller's W3C trace context; MCP did not, so an agent's question could not be correlated with the engine's audit record. `mcp_post` now owns one `mcp.request` span per request, made a child of `traceparent` through a new `propagation::adopt_trace_context_from_headers` (set parent + record ids; the existing helper only attached to the current span), and runs the dispatch inside it, so `QueryHandler`'s `current_trace_id()` and the audit line carry the caller's trace id; every successful JSON-RPC result carries it back as `result._meta.trace_id`, and a request without the header gets a fresh id (uuid when no OTel layer is installed, otherwise the root span's). sqe-mcp links `sqe-metrics-core` with the `propagation` feature (the layering rule allows core; no third-party dependency added). Test under a real OTel layer: header trace id == executor-observed trace id == `_meta.trace_id`; without the header a fresh 32-hex id shared by statement and response. Docs: MCP reference, Audit attribution.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (bc): the limiters answer like rate limiters (`feat/mcp-rate-limit-429-retry-after`).** Fifteen parallel metadata calls from one agent hit the pre-auth limiter (10 per minute per (peer, bearer), burst = rate) and got a bare `{"error":"rate_limited"}` with no `Retry-After`; the client gave up. Now: `rate_limit.auth_burst` (default 20) sizes what one (peer, principal) may present at once, sustained rate unchanged (governor `allow_burst`); the MCP pre-auth 429 carries `Retry-After` (60 / `auth_attempts_per_minute`, rounded up: the bucket's refill interval, the longest a client can have to wait, since the auth error itself carries no time) and a body naming the limiter, the 60 s window, the wait and both knobs. The query and metadata limiters return a `RateLimitDenial` (limiter, knob, exact wait from governor's `NotUntil`) rendered as `ResourceExhausted` text ending in `retry after {n}s`; an MCP tool denial adds `structuredContent {error: rate_limited, retry_after_seconds}`. Tests: twelve presentations of one bearer pass at the defaults and the burst is finite; the 429's status, header and body; the structured tool denial. Configuration reference lists every knob and its env name.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (bb): `search_tables` pages, reports what it skipped, and one metadata tool call is one rate-limit charge (`feat/mcp-search-tables-paging-metadata-limiter`, #699).** Over the Glue root a search for `%` matched 697 tables, returned 200 with no way to the rest, and every one of its 135 `SHOW TABLES` was charged to `per_user_queries_per_minute` (120), so the tail was skipped at DEBUG and the agent saw a listing that looked complete; the quickstart had raised the limit to 1000 to get around it. Now: `QueryExecutor` has two lanes, `execute` (statements the caller asked for, query limiter) and `execute_metadata` (the `SHOW`s a tool issues, not charged) plus `admit_metadata_call` (one `MetadataRateLimiter` charge per metadata tool call, `metadata_per_user_per_minute`, 120 default); the coordinator executor implements both and `mcp_router` / `start_mcp_server` take the metadata limiter. `search_tables` pages by `(schema, table)` like `describe_schema` (`page_size` default 200, max 500, `next_page_token`), keeps `total_rows`, and returns `skipped_schemas: [{schema, reason}]` for a schema that failed to list or sat beyond the 200-schema walk cap. Tests: paging over the scripted fixture with the token round trip and a rejected token; 135 schemas through `call_tool` = one admission, zero query-lane charges, 271 metadata statements. The configuration reference says which knob covers which path. The quickstart's 1000/min workaround goes back to the default in !1078 once this merges.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (ba): the MCP metadata path steers agents to `describe_schema` (`feat/mcp-steer-describe-schema`).** First live agent over the Glue root made 21 `describe_table` calls where 3 `describe_schema` calls would have done, and could not tell a view from a table without one more call per name: nothing in the `initialize` instructions or the tool descriptions pointed at `describe_schema`, and `list_tables` / `search_tables` returned bare names. Now: the instructions (three sentences) say search_tables, then describe_schema, and that describe_table is for one table already chosen; the three descriptions say the same; `list_tables` is its own handler (`SHOW TABLES` + the `SHOW VIEWS` `describe_schema` already runs) and every row carries `table_type`; `search_tables` rows carry it too, from one `SHOW VIEWS` per schema that matched, not per schema walked. Unit tests on the text and the column. Next in the same series (one MR each): `search_tables` paging and one metadata-limiter charge per tool call instead of one query-limiter charge per `SHOW` (#699), 429 with Retry-After from the pre-auth limiter, `traceparent` on the MCP path, the FULL OUTER JOIN wrong result (#700).
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bh): the MCP listener has a bind address, and anonymous mode defaults to loopback (`fix/mcp-bind-address-and-enabled`, #671 #672).** First of the MCP security batch for production. `start_mcp_server` bound `0.0.0.0` unconditionally, so `--mcp-anonymous` on a laptop or bastion was an unauthenticated data endpoint on every interface. New `[mcp] bind_address` and `--mcp-bind <ip>`: empty resolves to `127.0.0.1` under `mode = "anonymous"` and `0.0.0.0` otherwise (`McpConfig::bind_ip`), an address that does not parse fails `validate`, and the server warns when an anonymous endpoint is still network-reachable (explicit non-loopback bind, or `port = 0` on the web listener). #672: `is_enabled()` still honours `port > 0` alone for this release, but `enabled_by_port_only()` drives a startup DEPRECATED warning; the shortcut goes next release. Docs: getting-started/mcp-server.md, deployment/mcp.md, deployment/configuration.md. Tests: `mcp_bind_defaults_to_loopback_only_for_anonymous`, `mcp_bind_address_that_does_not_parse_fails_validation`, `mcp_port_only_is_flagged_as_the_deprecated_enable`. Verified: sqe-core mcp tests, sqe-server mcp tests, clippy on coordinator and server libs/bins.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-19 (bg): `tools/list` carries the MCP cache hints (`perf/mcp-tools-list-cache-hints`).** Work package 2 item 2. The result gains `ttlMs` (one hour) and `cacheScope: public` per SEP-2549 (spec 2026-07-28); the tool set is config-only, so the list a client caches is the list it would get again, and a client that honours the hints stops re-listing on every turn. Additive, older clients ignore the fields; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 2025-11-25. Test `tools_list_carries_cache_hints_and_is_stable`. Verified: sqe-mcp unit suite and clippy.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bj): MCP client registry and query rate limit are keyed on the caller's address as well as the user (`fix/mcp-peer-keyed-registry-and-limits`, #680; #676 analysed, left open).** Third of the MCP security batch. In `static` and `anonymous` mode every caller is one configured user, so the `Mcp-Session-Id` binding (user only) let one client relabel another's audit trail by replaying an observed id, and the per-user query limit was one shared global any single client could spend. `Session` gains `peer_ip` (set by MCP `authenticate` in every mode), `ClientSession` binds `(user, peer)` and `label`/`remove` check both, and `CoordinatorMcpExecutor` keys the query limiter on `peer|user`. No X-Forwarded-For handling was added: behind a proxy every caller shares the proxy address, which is today's behaviour, not a regression; trusted-proxy config is a follow-up if a proxied static deployment appears. #676 (scopes from an unverified decode): no introspection provider exists and `bearer_token` verifies the very JWT the decode reads, so there is no path where an opaque token passes the provider and 403s here; carrying scopes on `Identity` touches ~80 struct literals for a consumer that does not exist. Left open with that analysis for the provider that needs it. Tests: `the_same_principal_from_another_address_cannot_borrow_a_session_id`, `sessions_carry_the_peer_address_for_the_rate_limit_key`. Verified: workspace `cargo check`, sqe-mcp unit suite, clippy on the touched crates.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bl): the MCP listener has a request deadline, a graceful shutdown and a supervised certificate reloader (`fix/mcp-listener-timeout-shutdown`, #679).** Fifth of the MCP security batch. `mcp_router` wraps the routes in a deadline of `request_timeout_secs` + 15 s (90 s at the default; off when the statement deadline is 0), answering 504 with a JSON-RPC error, so the token check, the metadata fan-out and the encoding are bounded too, not only the statement. `start_mcp_server` takes a `CancellationToken` (re-exported from tokio-util): SIGTERM/SIGINT cancel it, `axum::serve` drains via `with_graceful_shutdown`, the TLS branch via `axum_server::Handle::graceful_shutdown(30 s)`, `supervise_listener` logs that exit at INFO instead of ERROR, and `watch_mcp_listener` no longer exits 70 for a drained listener. The `spawn_cert_reload_watch` handle was dropped; a watcher now logs at ERROR if the reloader ends or panics while the server is up. The global semaphore half of #678 is not here: !1083 (one metadata admission per tool call) covers the rate-limiter collision, and a process-wide probe bound is its own item. Tests: `watch_mcp_listener_does_not_exit_when_the_listener_closed_on_shutdown`; the it call site updated. Verified: sqe-server mcp tests, clippy on coordinator and server.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bi): MCP withholds system error text from the model (`fix/mcp-error-redaction`, #675).** Second of the MCP security batch. `call_tool`, the `list_catalogs` probes and the `describe_schema` per-table probe returned `error.to_string()`: object-store and catalog errors carry bucket names, object keys, hostnames and region or account hints, and an agent transcript is a leaky place for those. `client_error_text` now decides by `SqeErrorCode`: a user error (syntax, not-found, denial, not-supported, cancelled) goes through verbatim as on Flight and Trino; a system error becomes the code's `generic_message` plus an `error_id`, and the full text is logged at WARN under that id. Two system texts stay verbatim because the hint is the fix: the Arrow decimal overflow (value and precision only) and a rate-limit denial (retry-after). `list_catalogs` still classifies `auth_error` on the full text, then reports the redacted detail. Root-cause fix alongside in sqe-core: DataFusion's `Schema error: No field named x` was classified `ExecutionFailed`, so a bad column reference read as a system fault on every front door; it is `ColumnNotFound` now. Docs: deployment/mcp.md "Error text" section, tool description. Tests: `system_error_text_is_withheld_from_the_client_and_user_errors_are_not`, `error_code_column_not_found_from_datafusion_schema_error`, two existing tests updated to the redacted shape. Verified: sqe-mcp and sqe-core unit suites, clippy.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-20 (bk): `execute_sql_change` has an explicit surface (`fix/mcp-change-tool-allow-list`, #677).** Fourth of the MCP security batch. `ensure_change` only required "not read-only", and the catalog gate is inactive with `write_catalogs` and `hive.roots` both empty, so GRANT, REVOKE, DENY, policy and tag DDL, secrets, ATTACH/DETACH, SET SESSION, transactions and CALL all passed a tool described as a change tool. `is_change_surface` lists what the tool runs: data statements and table, schema and view DDL (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE, CREATE/CTAS/DROP/ALTER/RENAME TABLE, TRUNCATE, ANALYZE, COMMENT, partition and branch DDL, MSCK REPAIR, CREATE/DROP SCHEMA and VIEW); everything else is refused by the tool with a pointer at Flight SQL and Trino HTTP, fail closed on kinds it does not list. No admin toggle: add one when a deployment asks. Tool description and the book's write section say the same. Tests: `change_tool_refuses_administration_statements`; the procedure fail-closed test became `change_tool_refuses_a_procedure_before_the_catalog_gate` because a procedure no longer reaches the gate. Verified: sqe-mcp unit suite and clippy.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-19 (bf): Hive listings are process-wide per catalog, so an MCP tool call no longer re-lists Glue (`perf/hive-shared-listings`).** Work package 2 item 1. `HiveListings` lived on the session's `HiveCatalogProvider`, and an MCP tool call is a session of its own, so every `describe_schema` and every page of `search_tables` paid one `GetTables` per database it touched regardless of how fresh the previous call's snapshot was (135 schemas = 135 listings per call). The snapshots now live in `HiveMetadataCache` keyed by catalog name (`listings_for`), shared by every session's provider; a Hive listing is not principal-filtered and the `caller` gate runs at table load, so nothing about authorization moved. `invalidate`, `invalidate_database` and `invalidate_all` on the cache drop the listing too, which is what keeps a session's own `CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE` / `DROP TABLE` visible now that a fresh provider no longer means an empty snapshot; the coordinator passed a hard-coded 60 s as the listing TTL and now passes `[hive] metadata_ttl_secs`, whose default moves from 60 to 600 (Glue has no change token cheaper than the fetch, so keying the entry by `VersionId` would cost the `GetTable` it exists to avoid; an unchanged refetch already keeps the file facts). Test `sessions_share_one_listing_per_catalog`. Verified: provider unit and integration suites, sqe-core config tests, lib clippy on catalog/coordinator/core (the coordinator test target does not compile because of an in-progress `scheduler.rs` edit from another session, not touched here).
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (az): Athena views over a Glue root answered zero rows: base tables took the Iceberg policy branch (`fix/hive-view-scans-policy-key`, MR !1080).** First SQL day on the mcp-ranger-okta quickstart (Okta login from Claude Code, Ranger grants per Okta group, 135 databases live from Glue): every `bi_views` view returned 0 rows while its base tables read fine by their three-part names. The log said `Tag state unknown (cache miss or disabled); denying access` once per base table: a view body names its tables as the author wrote them (`db.t`, `awsdatacatalog.db.t`), DataFusion keeps that spelling in the `TableScan`, `resolve_policy_key` keys a scan without a catalog as Iceberg, and the unknown tag state denies fail-closed with `lit(false)`. `qualify_view_scans` now rewrites every scan of a planned view body to `{root}.{db}.{table}` (Athena's `awsdatacatalog` and the view's own catalog mean this root) before optimisation, so the Hive branch and the Ranger grants written against `{root}.{db}` apply; a unit test asserts both the premise and the result. Same session, same quickstart: a placeholder `[catalog]` URL made every SQL session fail while metadata tools worked (!1078, empty table + config assertion), `search_tables` burned one rate-limit token per database and silently dropped the tail (#699), an Okta access-policy rule that listed only device_code showed "You are not allowed to access this app" (documented), and a FULL OUTER JOIN over a windowed CTE returned one side (#700, needs the SQL). Not yet read on the live stack: the fixed view path, which needs the image rebuilt from main once !1080 merges.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-18 (ay): ponytail over-engineering audit applied, eleven branches (`refactor/audit-687-shuffle-tree` .. `refactor/audit-696-deps-features-gate`, `docs/audit-697-metrics-core-adoption`), umbrella #698.** Deleted the unbuilt worker-shuffle/exchange tree and governor admission (~4750 lines), three dead planner operator modules, the orphaned catalog modules, the unwired device-code flow, the duplicated circuit breaker (one in sqe-core), the second `sqe-worker` binary (compose files now run `sqe-server --mode worker`), dead paths across coordinator/write/lineage/sql/bench/quack, six orphan scripts, and ~25 unused dependency edges; `make deps-check` (cargo-machete over crates/sqe-*) and `make metrics-layering-check` keep them gone. Three audit claims were wrong and are recorded as kept: sqe-server's `glue` feature gates code, `session_props` drives SHOW SESSION, and the sqe-metrics-core split is already adopted (#697 is a plan + guard). Verified per branch: clippy -D warnings with tests on the touched crates and their unit tests; one `make test-integration` on the merged tree before the MRs.
> **STATUS 2026-09-17 (ay): MCP review, 14 issues, first fix (`perf/mcp-encoder-and-limit-injection`, #670).** A security, performance and well-architected review of the MCP server (core crate, coordinator wiring, config, quickstart, Helm, docs, tests) filed #670 to #683 under the new `app::mcp` label. Verdict: the request path is sound (OAuth default, writes off, constant-time static compare, anonymous refused under production or with an IdP, Origin fail-closed, 1 MiB body cap, every tool through the policy-enforced handler); the gaps sit at the edges. First fix in suggested order, #670: `batches_json` re-serialized the whole accumulated row array after every appended row to check `max_response_bytes` (quadratic in the row cap) and now accounts bytes incrementally against the framing cost; and `execute_sql_readonly` appends `LIMIT max_result_rows + 1` to a plain query that carries no LIMIT, FETCH or FOR clause and does not end in a comment, so the coordinator plans a bounded scan instead of collecting up to `query.max_result_rows` (a million rows by default) for a thousand-row answer. `total_rows` reads the cap plus one when it fires ("more than the cap"); tool description and the MCP page say so. Text append rather than an AST print because the executor must see the original text (time-travel clauses are stripped from the classification copy). NEXT in order: #671 bind address, #672 port-only enable, #673 in-crate router tests, #674 Helm, #675 error text, #676 provider-sourced scopes.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-17 (az): a LIMIT over a thousand-partition snapshot table reads one partition, and a query no longer outlives its caller (`perf/hive-partition-scan-at-scale`, #685 #686).** The Glue crawl's mcp.log showed four statements on `afas_raw.mgtinfo_ledger` (548k objects, 551 GB, 1,241 daily `snapshot_date` projected partitions) and `mgtinfo_accounts` (1,590 partitions) failing with "Query timed out after 300s" after 21 to 27 minutes each, retried by the sidecar so two ran side by side, RSS 335 MB to 1.8 GB. Three causes, all in the scan path, none in the data: `scan_from_entries` built one `ListingTable` per surviving partition at plan time and sequentially (each build lists the prefix, 0.65 s from this laptop, so 17 minutes before the first row); `EngineHiveDataStoreFactory::store_for` built a fresh S3 store per partition, and with no static key that is a fresh SDK credential chain loading the profile and resolving the SSO session 1,241 times per query (also the blocking work that starved the 300 s timer); and the plan held every partition's file metadata. Now: `LazyPartitionScanExec` takes a pushed-down LIMIT over more than one partition, sorts entries descending (newest first, the useful answer for a snapshot table and one a bare LIMIT never promised otherwise), builds and reads each partition's scan at execute time through the shared `plan_partition_scan`, and stops when the limit is met; the unlimited path and the worker-task listing run sixteen partition listings in flight instead of one after another; the factory caches one store per (bucket, secret) for five minutes. Provider test on the counting store: `LIMIT 1` over two projected partitions lists exactly one, `LIMIT 2` continues into the second, `count(*)` still reads all. Coordinator side: `mcp.request_timeout_secs` (default 75, below the ~90 s an MCP client waits before retrying) drops the statement's future past the deadline and reports it with the two ways out; `AbandonedQueryGuard` in `execute_inner` moves a dropped query's tracker record to Canceled/QUERY_ABANDONED and fires its cancel token, so a disconnected caller no longer leaves a "running" query for hours; `QueryTracker::finish` drops the second verdict the timeout arm and the shared error path both recorded, so a failure is logged once. Gotcha for the next reader: a `StreamExt::map` closure returning `async move` over a borrowed entry compiles in the catalog crate and breaks the coordinator's `Send` proof ("implementation of `Send` is not general enough"); build the futures with `Iterator::map` first and hand the Vec to `stream::iter(..).buffered(..)`. Measured against live Glue with the final release build beside the old server: `SELECT * FROM mgtinfo_accounts LIMIT 10` 21 min → 6.8 s cold, 1.4 s warm, `EXPLAIN` shows `LazyPartitionScanExec: partitions=2270, limit=10, order=newest-first`; `mgtinfo_ledger LIMIT 5` (gz JSON, 548k objects) 0.7 s; an unlimited `count(*)` over the ledger listed all 1,241 partitions in about 20 s (was 13 min of listing alone) and then failed honestly on a 2023 object in GLACIER storage class, which is the table's state, not the engine's; `count(*) WHERE snapshot_date >= DATE '2026-01-01'` (260 partitions, ~110 GB gz JSON) hit the MCP deadline at 80 s with the tracker record `CANCELED / QUERY_ABANDONED` and nothing left running, RSS 3.3 GB at that point (the eager plan holds 260 partitions' file metadata plus decode buffers; a lever for later). Zero 429s in the new build's log across all of it. The live run found two more causes under the first three. (4) The SDK default chain does not cache credentials when called outside an SDK client (aws-config 1.x moved the cache into the service client's identity resolver), so `SdkObjectStoreCredentials` asked the SSO portal for role credentials on EVERY S3 request; sixteen partition listings in flight answered `TooManyRequestsException: HTTP 429` within seconds, surfacing as "storage credentials expired". Now cached single-flight until five minutes before expiry (fifteen minutes without one); unit tests count one resolution for 32 concurrent callers. The old server still running beside the new one keeps flooding the portal until it is restarted, and its 429s hit the new build too. (5) `detect_compression` recorded a FAILED listing (that 429) as "uncompressed" in the facts cache, so a gzipped JSON table failed for the next minute as `Encountered unexpected ''` from the JSON parser; a failed probe now records nothing and the next lookup probes again (provider test with a store that fails one listing). What the crawler should know is now in the engine (newest partition first), so the sidecar needs no snapshot-aware logic for `sample_rows`; `describe_schema` could still expose partition keys so a profile can say "one day of 1,241".
>

> **STATUS 2026-09-17 (ba): the Glue database scope, hardened (`feat/glue-database-scope-hardening`).** The ask was a command-line way to select which databases a Glue-backed MCP server exposes; it already existed as `--glue-database a,b` / `[[hive.roots]] databases = [...]` and worked live (`SHOW SCHEMAS FROM glue` listed only the two named, a table elsewhere was "not found"). Two gaps closed: the scope compared case-sensitively against Glue's lower-cased names, so `--glue-database Claude` listed a schema no query could resolve (now folded, trimmed, deduplicated; `database_blocked` compares case-insensitively); and a misspelt name was served as an empty schema forever (now each scoped name is checked with `GetDatabase` at the first listing, a missing one is a WARN and not listed, a Glue call that fails leaves it served unverified). Docs on three pages say what this is: a visibility scope shared by every caller of the endpoint, not per-user authorization. Found while verifying and NOT fixed here: `glue.information_schema.tables` on a Glue root returns the system catalog's schemas (jdbc, metadata, runtime) and zero Hive tables, another face of #664/#665; the issue could not be filed because the GitLab API was returning HTML error pages, file it first thing. NEXT, in the order the user set: (1) Okta in front of the MCP endpoint, which is the existing `bearer_token` provider with Okta's JWKS/issuer/audience and `mcp.auth.mode = "oauth"` (the anonymous flag then becomes refused by config validation, as designed), plus the `oauth_scopes` mapping for `sqe:mcp:read`; (2) the Ranger engine for per-user table access on the Glue root, where a Hive table is readable only after a Ranger access policy grants `select` on `<root>.<database>`, so the database scope becomes the coarse fence and Ranger the per-user gate. Neither needs new engine code to start; both need a config file instead of the flag-only path.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (ax): a decimal the metastore declared too narrow says so (`fix/hive-decimal-narrower-than-data-message`).** The sidecar's profile of one Glue table failed on `1029.23 is too large to store in a Decimal128 of precision 5. Max is 999.99`: Glue declares the column `decimal(5,2)`, the Parquet file (Trino-written) carries `decimal(10,2)`, one row of 13,816 exceeds the declaration. SQE reads at the declared type, as Athena does, so that row fails every statement touching the column. Decided against reading the footer to let the file's type win: it would add a storage round trip per table to the crawl we just made Glue-only, and make the same column answer differently in SQE and Athena. Instead the overflow classifies as `TYPE_MISMATCH` (a user-facing class, detail passes through) and the MCP failure text gains a hint naming the declared precision and the fix (widen the Glue column type; `describe_table` shows which column). The column name itself is not in Arrow's message; the hook that could add it is DataFusion 55's `PhysicalExprAdapterFactory` on the file scan (`SchemaAdapter` is gone), which is where a per-column wrapped cast would live if the hint proves insufficient. Also measured tonight: the MCP slowness during the crawl was the laptop, not the server: load average 46 with Microsoft Defender at 500 percent CPU over the cargo `target/` tree during a release build; `SELECT 1` was 1.1 s under that and 33 ms a minute later.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (aw): `describe_schema` queryability without touching storage (`perf/describe-schema-queryability-without-listing`).** With `include_queryability` the tool planned `EXPLAIN SELECT * FROM t LIMIT 1` per table, and planning a Hive table lists its S3 prefix (DataFusion's `ListingTable`, list cache off since #363): half a second warm, one to two seconds cold, a hang with expired credentials; 40 tables cost 5 s and a 25-table page hung. `LIMIT 0` gives the same answer for what a crawler asks: DataFusion resolves the table (that is where `resolve_format`, the SerDe allow-list, the location boundary and the column typing run, and where a view's SQL is planned) and eliminates the zero limit before physical planning, so no scan and no listing. A provider test on the counting store pins it: after the one facts listing, `EXPLAIN ... LIMIT 0` adds zero LIST calls while `LIMIT 1` adds one. What still lists cold is the size-only statistics listing in `table()` (once per manifest version, cached 60 s), so a first crawl of 618 tables pays 618 LISTs at eight in flight (about half a minute) and the second one pays none. Next lever if that matters: a TTL-bounded DataFusion list-files cache for Hive roots, invalidated on SQE's own writes, so `SELECT`, `sample_rows` and `EXPLAIN` within the TTL skip the LIST too; not a startup warmer over every table, which would list 618 prefixes a minute for tables nobody reads.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (av): storage credentials that expire say so, and are not frozen into the process (`fix/storage-credentials-expiry`).** After a few hours every scan on a Glue-backed table failed with `Object Store error: Generic S3 error: an error occurred while loading credentials` while `SELECT 1` still worked. Two findings. The refreshing provider already existed: with no static key, S3 stores read through the AWS SDK default chain (`aws_config::sdk_object_store_credentials`), which caches and refreshes for as long as the SSO or role session that issued the credentials lasts; that message is the chain saying the session itself ended. The trap was the environment path: `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` + `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` (the `export-credentials` recipe the docs showed) were pinned into `[storage]` as static keys at startup, session token included, and expired on a clock nobody could see. Now: temporary env keys are not pinned (the SDK chain reads the same environment while valid; `AWS_PROFILE` gets refresh), a static key with a session token under `[storage]` warns at startup; the SDK bridge renders `storage credentials expired or unavailable (aws sdk default chain): <chain>` with the remedy and `sqe-core` classifies that text and S3's `ExpiredToken` / `InvalidAccessKeyId` / "security token ... invalid" replies as `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS_UNAVAILABLE` (65605, Trino `EXTERNAL`, gRPC `Unauthenticated`), ahead of the 403 patterns that used to make it look like the user's grant; and the MCP `list_catalogs` diagnostics gain an `object_store` entry (`provider`, `status` ok/auth_error/unknown/timeout, `expires_at` when the SDK knows it) from a probe the coordinator wires from `[storage]`, so the sidecar checks before a long job. Tests: env adoption (permanent pinned, temporary not), the message, the classification, the MCP entry. Docs on three pages. Same branch, same cause: `describe_schema` over 25 Hive tables hung indefinitely with `include_stats` or `include_queryability` on (0.4 s with both off) because a stats or plan probe on a table whose storage credentials had expired waited instead of failing; every per-table statement now has a 20 s deadline (`DESCRIBE_SCHEMA_PROBE_TIMEOUT`) and a timed-out probe lands on that table's `stats_error` / `queryability_error` with a pointer to the diagnostics, and the SDK credential resolution itself is bounded at 10 s (`CREDENTIAL_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT`) so a chain that hangs becomes the same `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS_UNAVAILABLE`. A hang test in the `describe_schema` fixture (a probe that never answers, paused tokio clock) pins it.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (au): the last two hidden Athena views, from their bodies this time (`fix/glue-view-lambda-dialect-and-scalar-subquery`).** `No field named tag` was not a lambda the engine lacks: `filter(tags, (tag) -> starts_with(tag, 'drt:'))` runs fine as a user statement, because the engine session parses with the DuckDB dialect; the view-planning scratch session was built from a bare `SessionConfig`, so the generic dialect read `->` as the JSON arrow and planned `tag` as a column. The scratch session now uses `session_tuning::engine_session_config()` (the generic-dialect retry for `UNNEST` table factors stays). `Correlated scalar subquery must be aggregated to return at most one row` was Athena's lookup idiom `(SELECT display_name FROM user WHERE id = sa.manager_id)` twice in one view; the front door now wraps that projection in `max()` when the subquery is correlated (its WHERE qualifies a column with a name that is none of its own tables or aliases), projects one non-aggregate item and has no DISTINCT/GROUP BY/HAVING/LIMIT/WITH; DataFusion's own alternative, `LIMIT 1`, does not plan either (`Physical plan does not support logical expression ScalarSubquery`). Tests: the compat rewrite (positive, unqualified inner column, and five shapes left alone) and a provider test planning both idioms as views over the CSV fixture from a plain context. Also measured on the live server: `max()` gives the same rows as the raw lookup would on a key match.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (at): what an agent can rely on through `execute_sql_readonly`, measured and written down (`docs/mcp-agent-functions`).** The sidecar's list, checked against a release build over Glue-backed Parquet: `sha256(lower(col))` (SQE's own function, HMAC under `[policy] mask_key`, plain SHA-256 without it), `digest(col, 'sha256')` (Binary), both on `Utf8` and `Utf8View`; `INTERSECT` of two `DISTINCT` hash sets works and needs `IS NOT NULL` on both sides because `sha256(NULL)` is `NULL` and `INTERSECT` matches `NULL` with `NULL` (verified: one spurious overlap without it); `COUNT(DISTINCT)` and `approx_distinct` both work on Boolean, Int32/64, every Decimal128 precision present, Date32, Timestamp(µs), Utf8, Utf8View, List and Struct, agreeing within 1 percent; `get_table_stats` is `SHOW STATS FOR` and reports Glue's `numRows`/`totalSize` when a crawler or `ANALYZE` left them, the planner's listed size when Trino wrote `-1`. Item 4 of the same list, the correlated scalar subquery on a CTE alias that failed with `No field named s.<col>`, planned and ran in eleven variants on this build (DISTINCT, GROUP BY, ORDER BY LIMIT, a join in the CTE, two subqueries, `s.*` over struct/list columns); the one shape that fails is a non-equality correlation on a second outer column (`Physical plan does not support logical expression ScalarSubquery`), a DataFusion decorrelation limit, documented with the rewrite. The exact SQL that failed for the sidecar is still needed to go further. All of it is one new section on the MCP page, "Functions available to agents".
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (as): an expired AWS session says so (`fix/glue-credential-errors`).** When the engine's credentials for a Glue root lapse, `list_schemas` answered `hive_external: object store error: glue get_databases failed: dispatch failure`: the SDK's `Display` is the bare error kind and the reason ("no credentials", "the SSO session has expired") sits two levels down its `source()` chain. `GlueMetadataSource` now classifies every failed call: a `DispatchFailure` whose chain talks about credentials, a token, SSO or a provider, or a signed request STS rejects (`UnrecognizedClientException`, `InvalidSignatureException`, `ExpiredTokenException`, `InvalidClientTokenId`), becomes `HiveError::Credentials` reading `AWS credentials missing or expired for the Glue catalog (region=eu-central-1, op=get_databases: ...)` with the remedy; every other failure keeps the operation name and the full chain instead of "dispatch failure". `AccessDeniedException` stays a permission error on purpose. The MCP `list_catalogs` tool adds `diagnostics`: one entry per visible Hive root probed with `SHOW SCHEMAS` (concurrent, 5 s bound) as `reachable` / `auth_error` / `timeout` / `unreachable` with the detail, so the sidecar's journey page can say "the catalog is fine, the engine's credentials are not". Also verified: a scan with unusable S3 keys fails loudly (`403 InvalidAccessKeyId` on the LIST, planning error), on the `read_parquet` path at least; the silent-zero shape from 2026-09-02 (sidecar bridge, expired session token) did not reproduce with invalid static keys and stays open until a real expiry is caught with `RUST_LOG=debug`.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (ar): a dead `/mcp` listener is no longer a live PID (`fix/mcp-listener-supervision`).** The sqe-semantic sidecar saw `POST /mcp` stop answering (curl exit 000, no TCP accept) twice on 2026-09-16 after 30 to 60 minutes of idle time while `pgrep sqe-server` still listed the process. The code allowed exactly that: in `--mcp-only` mode `main` only awaits a signal, and the listener was a detached `tokio::spawn` whose `JoinHandle` was dropped, so an accept-loop exit or panic left nothing running and nobody told. Now `start_mcp_server` binds first (reporting the bound port, so port 0 works in tests), runs the serve loop under `supervise_listener` (ERROR log on return, error or panic; an `AliveGuard` clears the liveness flag on any exit including abort), and returns the task; `sqe-server` keeps the handle in `watch_mcp_listener`, which flips readiness and exits 70 (`EX_SOFTWARE`) when the task ends. `/healthz` answers `503 mcp listener down` and `/readyz` carries `checks.mcp` for a dedicated port. Tests: healthz/readyz on a dead flag, the watcher's exit path, and `tests/it/mcp_listener_supervision_e2e` (real TCP, initialize + tools/call, idle 65 s past the 60 s session-context TTL, again, then abort clears the flag and closes the port). Root cause of the original hang is still open: a repro instance of the release build has idled with `RUST_LOG=debug` and a one-minute probe since 16:49 and has answered every probe so far; if it reproduces, `idle.log` in the session scratchpad has the last debug lines before it.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (aq): `/api/v1/status` tells the truth about the build and what it serves (`fix/status-real-versions-and-serving`).** The endpoint reported `datafusionVersion: "51"` on a DataFusion 55 binary: a string literal in three places that nobody bumped through three upgrades. It now reads `datafusion::DATAFUSION_VERSION`, and a `serving` block says what the process actually answers, from the effective config after flags: `mode` (`mcp-only` when every SQL door is closed and `/mcp` is open, else `full`), the three SQL ports or `null`, `mcp` (`port`, `authMode` oauth/static/anonymous, `allowWrites`) or `null`, `policyEngine`, and every attached catalog with its kind (`rest`/`hms`/`glue`/`jdbc`/`s3tables`, `hive-glue`/`hive-sidecar`). Two unit tests: the flag-only `--glue-catalog --mcp-anonymous --mcp-only` shape, and the JSON. Groundwork for the next step, which is making the ops port report a dead `/mcp` listener (the sidecar saw the MCP port stop answering on a live PID twice on 2026-09-16; a detached `tokio::spawn` with a dropped `JoinHandle` is where the listener lives today).
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (ap): the Glue metadata path behind the MCP crawler (`perf/mcp-glue-metadata-path`).** A timed sweep of every MCP read tool against a Glue database on the release build found three costs that were not the data: (1) every `SHOW COLUMNS`, `SHOW STATS` and `SHOW CREATE TABLE` on a Hive root fetched the manifest from Glue again (60 to 80 ms per statement, `GetTable`) while the planner answered the same table from its cache, so the DDL handler now reads through the same three-tier cache (`manifest_cached`, mirroring `manifest_for`); (2) `describe_schema` asked `information_schema.tables` for the table kinds, which enumerates every database of the catalog (135 databases, 12.6 s cold for one call), so `SHOW VIEWS FROM <catalog>.<schema>` exists (classifier variant, Hive root from manifests, Iceberg from REST `list_views`) and the tool uses it; (3) `search_tables` ran one `SHOW TABLES` per database serially (135 × ~110 ms = 17 s), now eight in flight in schema order. Also: Hive `SHOW STATS` reports the planner's listed byte size when Glue carries `totalSize = -1` (every Trino/Athena-written table does). Measured before: describe_schema cold 23.9 s / warm 6.0 s, search_tables 17.3 s; after: see the MR. What remains is the data path: each `EXPLAIN` or `SELECT ... LIMIT 1` on a Glue table lists its S3 prefix on every plan (DataFusion's `ListingTable`, list-files cache disabled since #363), ~500 ms warm.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (ao): metadata statements leave the per-user memory budget alone (`fix/admission-metadata-no-memory-reservation`).** The first `describe_schema` run on the release build against a 40-table Glue database returned 36 tables with `Per-user memory budget exceeded ... 1073741824 bytes reserved, limit 1073741824`: the tool runs eight `SHOW COLUMNS` / `SHOW STATS` / `EXPLAIN` statements at once, admission reserved the full 256MB `max_query_memory` for each, and the 1GB `per_user_memory_budget` admits exactly four. Exactly four tables succeeded per run, every run. Admission now classifies the leading keyword before the parse and skips the memory reservation for `SHOW`, `DESCRIBE`/`DESC` and `EXPLAIN` (not `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`, which executes); they still take both concurrency permits. Two unit tests on the classifier; the crawler timing is re-measured on the rebuilt binary.
>
> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (an): two more Athena view idioms (`fix/glue-view-planning-unicode-split`).** The restarted server's log named six views still hidden. Two were fixable from the error alone: Trino serializes any literal holding a non-ASCII character as `U&'...'` (an en dash in a `concat`), which sqlparser decodes and DataFusion's planner rejects as an unknown `Value` variant, so the front door's AST pass now turns it into a plain literal; and `split(s, delimiter, limit)` failed because `split` was an alias on DataFusion's `string_to_array`, whose third argument is a null-marker string, so `sqe-trino-functions` now owns a `split` UDF with Trino semantics (empty delimiter splits into characters, the last element holds the rest under a limit). The other four need the view bodies: `No field named tag` under an `UNNEST` (two views) and `Correlated scalar subquery must be aggregated to return at most one row` (one view); the fourth is the `split` one's sibling. Verified: sqe-sql 542, sqe-trino-functions 117+110+66, hive_provider_test 41, clippy clean.

> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (am): MCP `describe_schema`, one call per schema for the crawler (`feat/mcp-describe-schema`).** The sqe-semantic sidecar crawled a 40-table Glue database in ~70 s because it made three metadata calls per table plus a profile statement over MCP. The new tool describes every table and view in a schema in one call, composed of the statements the single-table tools issue so it is never looser than they are: `SHOW TABLES` (visibility-filtered; the Iceberg branch now also lists REST views, as Trino does), `SHOW COLUMNS FROM` per table (the policy path `describe_table` uses), `SHOW STATS FOR` when `include_stats` (snapshot summary, or for a Hive root the manifest's `numRows`/`recordCount`/`totalSize`, new: `SHOW STATS` used to fail on Hive tables), `EXPLAIN SELECT * .. LIMIT 1` when `include_queryability` (planning only), and one `information_schema.tables` read per call for table-vs-view (best effort). Bounded concurrency (8), per-table errors recorded on the entry (`error` / `stats_error` / `queryability_error`) rather than failing the call, pages by table name (200 default, 500 cap) with the byte cap shortening a page and `next_page_token` pointing at the first table dropped. `SHOW COLUMNS` / `DESCRIBE` gained a `comment` column (Glue column comment; Iceberg `doc` via a cached load) for both tools. `SHOW STATS FOR` now splits quoted names, so `get_table_stats` works on `"cat"."schema"."t"`. Not included: table `properties`, column-level statistics, `snapshot_id` (always null). Verified: 4 new sqe-mcp tests (pagination, error isolation, kind/stats/plan fields, describe_table parity on the same statement), sqe-mcp and coordinator clippy, `cargo check --locked --no-default-features --bin sqe-server`; integration test `mcp_describe_schema_e2e` for `make test-integration FILTER=mcp_describe_schema`. Timing on the live Glue database needs the user's server restarted on this build.

> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (al): Ranger access policies gate Hive-root tables, deny by default (`feat/ranger-hive-access-default-deny`).** Designing the company rollout (Okta groups in the JWT, Ranger for the model, Glue root read with a Fargate task role) surfaced the inversion: SQE read only mask and row-filter policies from the Ranger `hive` service, because object-level access is Polaris's job for Iceberg, so with no Polaris in front of a Glue root every table was open to every authenticated user until someone wrote a mask, and a new Glue table was exposed the moment it appeared. `RangerStore` now parses `policyItems`/`denyPolicyItems`/`allowExceptions`/`denyExceptions` and, for a namespace whose first component is a registered Hive root, requires an enabled access policy granting `select`/`all` (deny items win, exceptions carve out, `column` must be `*` or absent for table access, Ranger's `public` group matches everyone); no grant = the existing deny-all sentinel, so listings, `SHOW CREATE TABLE`, `information_schema`, MCP tools and `SELECT` all read "table not found" through the plumbing that already existed. Iceberg namespaces are untouched. `[policy.ranger] hive_access_policies` (default true) turns it off; the coordinator logs which way it is wired at startup; the group-only-item warning now counts access items and skips `public`. Not modelled: `policyPriority` override policies, column-level access decisions (grant the table, mask the columns). Verified: 292 sqe-policy lib tests (10 new), clippy clean on sqe-core/sqe-policy/sqe-coordinator. Not measured against a live Ranger yet; the manual check is one allow policy on `<root>.<database>` for a group, then `SHOW TABLES` as a member and as a non-member.

> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (ak): Glue always compiled into `sqe-server` (`feat/glue-default-in-server`).** The Glue Hive root was an opt-in cargo feature, and every shipping build site (`release-binaries`, the Dockerfile, `scripts/build.sh`) passes `--no-default-features`, so a stock release binary or image refused `--glue-catalog` while the local dev build accepted it. `sqe-server` now enables `sqe-coordinator/glue` on the dependency itself, so the flag-less builds carry it (`cargo tree --no-default-features -i aws-sdk-glue` shows it reachable from the binary); the `glue` feature stays as a no-op alias. HMS/JDBC stay behind `CATALOG_FEATURES=sqe-catalog/full-backends`. Docs (mcp-server getting-started, production guide) updated. Follow-ups from the same production-design conversation, both unscheduled: SQE ignores Ranger policyType-0 access policies, so without Polaris there is no table-level allow/deny for Hive roots (a `false` row filter is the only deny); and the MCP door drops the peer address before the query span (`client_ip: None` on every MCP query, no `X-Forwarded-For` either).

> **STATUS 2026-09-16 (aj): the two Athena views that stayed hidden (`fix/glue-view-planning-context`).** Live on the merged !1038-!1040 binary, one of the catalog's three views planned (161 rows) but the other two still answered "not found". Their bodies (fetched with `aws glue get-table` from the user's shell) showed why: view planning ran in a bare DataFusion context. Fixes: `plan_view` registers `sqe_trino_functions` (base + extended) and runs the body through `SqlFrontDoor` + `rewrite_named_tvf_args` like a user statement; the DuckDB planner dialect has no `UNNEST` table factor (sqlparser gates it to BigQuery/Postgres/Generic), so a body that fails is re-parsed with `Dialect::Generic` before giving up (`CROSS JOIN UNNEST(SEQUENCE(..)) t (p)` now plans as a lateral unnest); new `sqe_sql::rewrite_trim_both_from` in the front door's fallback chain lowers `trim(BOTH FROM x)` (sqlparser rejects the char-less form) to `TRIM(x)`; `date_parse` fills unnamed fields Trino-style so `date_parse(period, '%Y%m')` works (chrono refused it). Probed with `sqe-cli --embedded -e` against 34 idioms from the two bodies; everything else already passed (from_iso8601_timestamp, AT TIME ZONE, bool_or, array_join, date_add, 1E1, scalar subqueries, quoted aliases). Not yet verified against the live views (server was down while this was built; the MCP `information_schema.columns` query I ran likely stalled it, that path still walks every table). Follow-up: the same UNNEST gap applies to user statements on the coordinator's DuckDB dialect; a per-statement generic-dialect retry there is the same three lines. Verified: hive_provider_test 41, sqe-sql 541, sqe-trino-functions 116, clippy clean on the three crates.

> **STATUS 2026-09-15 (ai): the MCP-over-Glue session's four issues, four MRs.** A live MCP session over a Glue catalog (debug build, `--mcp-only`) was slow and confusing; issues #666-#669 name the causes, and each has an MR. !1037 (#668 + #669): `QueryTracker::failed` writes one `Query failed` event (WARN for user errors, ERROR otherwise) with `query_id`, `error_code`, `elapsed_ms` and the full error, `complete` writes `Query completed`; three failed queries in the session log had no error line at all. `sqe-server` logs `version`, `git_sha`, `build_profile` (new `build.rs`), warns once on a debug build, and `/api/v1/status` reports the same. !1038 (#667): Athena views in Glue (`VIRTUAL_VIEW`, Presto payload in `ViewOriginalText`) were listed and then hidden as "storage format not readable"; the manifest now carries the text, `athena_view.rs` decodes it, and `HiveSchemaProvider::plan_view` plans the Trino SQL in a scratch session over the same catalog (default schema = the view's database, `awsdatacatalog` aliased) into a `ViewTable`; views over views work, unplannable views are hidden with the reason. !1039 (#666 caching, stacked on !1038): the 7.8 s `SHOW TABLES` was `HiveDdlHandler::list_tables` fetching 618 manifests one by one for the policy check; `HiveMetadataSource::list_manifests` makes Glue's `GetTables` the bulk fetch and warms the cache from the pre-plan refresh and the handler, `SchemaProvider::table_type` answers `information_schema.tables` from the manifest (no per-table build, no S3 LIST), an unchanged refetch keeps the file facts (`HiveManifest: PartialEq`), `[hive] metadata_ttl_secs` exposes the hard-coded 60 s. This branch (#666 timeouts + refresh, stacked on !1039): `TimeoutSource` bounds every metastore call at `[hive] metadata_timeout_secs` (default 10, Glue SDK clocks set too), `manifest_for` serves the stale entry with a WARN or fails with `catalog metadata timeout`; `CALL system.refresh_catalog_cache(table => .. | schema => ..)` drops Hive manifests for that scope, the admin refresh endpoint drops them all, and the MCP tool `refresh_metadata` wraps the CALL with a 10 s per-scope cooldown and a not-found hint on the lookup tools. Not measured live (no AWS creds in the agent shell); the manual check is the `SHOW TABLES` timing on the largest database and a `SELECT count(*)` on one of the Athena views. Local link note: the Mac's ld64.lld cannot read the macOS 26.5 SDK, link with `DEVELOPER_DIR=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools SDKROOT=.../MacOSX15.2.sdk`. Verified: clippy with tests on the touched crates; provider, tracker, procedures, MCP and server unit suites.

> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (ag): flag-only sqe-server + Glue-over-MCP, verified live (`feat/glue-mcp-flag-only`, off the #576 branch).** User wanted `aws sso login` then an MCP server over the current region's Glue tables with no config file, auth optional. Made it work and fixed three things found live against the SBP datalake Glue catalog (account 088044431914, 134 databases, all Hive external): (1) `sqe-server` starts from a built-in default config when no file is named and `sqe.toml` is absent (`DEFAULT_FLAG_ONLY_CONFIG`, shared `load_config_or_default` used by both `main` and `async_main`); new `--allow-s3-prefix` (repeatable) appends to `[storage.tvf]`. (2) The live Glue Hive root built its `aws_config` loader with no HTTP client, so the first GetDatabases panicked "a http_client is required" (the `default-https-client` feature is off because it hardcodes aws-lc); extracted `sqe_catalog::aws_config::sdk_config_loader()` with the ring HTTPS client and pointed `GlueMetadataSource::from_env` at it. (3) `list_catalogs`, `SHOW SCHEMAS FROM <root>` and `DESCRIBE <root>.<db>.<tbl>` did not handle Hive roots: list_catalogs omitted them, SHOW SCHEMAS returned "Unknown catalog", and DESCRIBE went through `information_schema.columns` which refreshes every catalog's listing snapshots (a live Glue catalog = minutes of GetTables per DESCRIBE, observed as a 60 s+ hang); added Hive-root branches (`HiveDdlHandler::list_databases` / `describe_table`, `hive_columns_batch`). Verified flag-only: `sqe-server --glue-catalog glue --allow-s3-prefix ... --mcp-anonymous --mcp-port 8100` with nothing on disk; list_catalogs=2, list_schemas=134, list_tables(claude)=6, describe(user_usage)=18 cols, count=81112, group-by top models, DROP refused. Follow-ups the user asked for, both shipped and verified live: `--allow-all-buckets` (config `[storage.tvf] allow_all_object_stores`) as the explicit allow-all switch (a bare `--allow-s3-prefix s3://` also works, confirmed reading a refined-datazone table not in the listed prefixes); and `--glue-database` (repeatable, comma-separated) scoping `--glue-catalog` to named databases (`GlueMetadataSource.databases`: `list_databases` returns exactly them, `list_tables`/`get` refuse others).
