> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (ae): SQE connects to RustFS S3 Tables (`feat/rest-catalog-client-side-sigv4`, stacked on the #382 MR).** Asked whether SQE can talk to the catalog in fdass/iceberg-rustfs (RustFS 1.0.0-rc.6 with its built-in Iceberg REST catalog). Out of the box it could not, for three separate reasons found one behind the other against a live container: RustFS requires SigV4 (service `s3`) but advertises nothing in `/v1/config`, so the vendored client sent unsigned requests (403 "Signature is required"); once signed, RustFS demanded the S3 payload hash header (400 "missing header: x-amz-content-sha256"), which the vendored signer never set; and SQE's own raw REST helper (`authorize_table_access` HEAD, `rest_request`, `fetch_table_etag_inner`) bypasses the vendored authenticator and sent `Authorization: Bearer ` with the empty bearer (400 "invalid header: authorization", surfaced as "Authorization check failed for table"). Fixes: `CatalogAuthConfig::Aws { signing_name, signing_region }` maps to the `rest.sigv4-enabled` / `rest.signing-*` props (`client_side_sigv4_props`, region defaults to `[storage] s3_region`); the vendored `SigV4Signer` is public, signs an `http::HeaderMap` (so the reqwest 0.13 raw path and the reqwest 0.12 vendored client share it), sets `PayloadChecksumKind::XAmzSha256` when the service is `s3`, and takes static keys from `s3.access-key-id` / `s3.secret-access-key` before the SDK chain; `SessionCatalog` builds the same signer from the same props and `authenticate_raw_request` signs or bearers every raw call. Live: SHOW SCHEMAS, CREATE SCHEMA, CREATE TABLE, INSERT, SELECT, CTAS, DROP TABLE all clean against `rustfs/rustfs:1.0.0-rc.6`. New `quickstart/rustfs-s3tables/` (compose with bucket-init and the `PUT /iceberg/v1/buckets/warehouse` enable call, sqe.toml, queries, run.sh --check, book page, index rows). Not visible to the raw path by design: SigV4 a server only advertises via `/v1/config` (Glue, Amazon S3 Tables keep the bearer on raw calls as before; the vendored client signs). Verified: clippy with tests on core, auth, catalog; the four sigv4 unit tests; config (273) and per_catalog (7) suites; slim `--no-default-features --features rest` check of sqe-catalog.

> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (ad): #382 audited against the code; the one missing check added (`fix/382-spill-bucket-isolation`, stacked on the #407 test MR).** Of the four items from the !710 review: quota under concurrency is a `fetch_update` CAS with a `QuotaHold` that refunds on abort and settles at finish (done, tested); `finish()` PUTs the final object once (done, `finish_puts_the_final_object_once`); the lifecycle-rule dependency is a startup `warn!` (done) and is now in the configuration reference (was undocumented); bucket isolation was the gap and is a `validate()` rule: `worker.spill.s3.bucket` may not be the bucket of any `[[hive.roots]]` location or `s3://` warehouse in the file. Also found: `validate()` still refused `backend = "s3"` as "not implemented yet" while `"tiered"` (which builds the same `S3SegmentStore`) passed, so the pure-S3 backend was unreachable by config and the tiered one reached it ungated; the refusal is replaced by "requires `worker.spill.s3.bucket`" for both. Two tests. Left on #382 (low): the S3 read path materializes a segment before use; process-local quota counters start at zero after a restart. Verified: clippy with tests on sqe-core; config tests.

> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (ac): #407 leftover, the slow-consumer pause gate runs (`test/407-slow-consumer-pause-gate`, stacked on the #412 fan-in MR).** The `#[ignore]`d placeholder in `crates/sqe-worker/tests/slow_consumer.rs` is a test: a `CountingStore` wrapper counts data GETs (every non-HEAD `get_opts`; `get_range(s)` lower to it), eight 2 MiB files with distinct payloads and dictionary encoding off (the harness's constant-payload generator produces 80 KiB files), a 4 MiB `ScanBudget` (fetch lane 1 MiB, per-file cap 512 KiB, so two open files at most), two batches taken then a 2 s pause. Asserted: GET count frozen across the pause, live ownership within budget, every row after the pause, and MORE GETs after the pause (the freeze was backpressure, not exhaustion), lanes at zero. 2.7 s wall. Left on #407: adaptive decode concurrency from outbound queue depth, and the `DoExchange` response path (#406, feature-gated off). Verified: clippy on the test target; the test three times.

> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (ab): #412 follow-up, the gate's fan-in comes from `target_partitions` (`fix/412-sort-gate-fan-in-from-config`, stacked on the write-gate MR).** `SortMemoryRule.max_fan_in` is `Option<usize>`; `None` reads `config.execution.target_partitions` in `optimize`, so the read path (`post_planning`) and the write path (`execute_source_stream`) size the merge budget the same way without either caller computing it. `with_max_fan_in` pins it for the planner's own tests. Consequence spelled out in the CHANGELOG: on the default 256 MiB grant the gate admits up to 1,024 partitions, so nothing changes for ordinary deployments; a small `query.max_query_memory` with a wide `target_partitions` is now refused instead of merging past the pool. Tests: `fan_in_defaults_to_target_partitions` (32 fits an 8 MiB grant, 64 does not, an explicit 8 wins), `admits_reasonable_grant` pinned to 8 partitions because `ConfigOptions::new()` follows the machine's core count. Verified: clippy with tests on planner, write, coordinator, server; planner sort tests; the write-gate unit test.

> **STATUS 2026-09-14 (aa): #412 first half, the sort admission gate on the write path (`fix/412-write-path-sort-admission`, stacked on the #665 MR).** Finding: `post_planning` documents that its rule list is deliberately not registered on the shared session state so the write path is not affected, and the write path had no gate of its own; `SortMemoryRule` only ever protected SELECT. `WriteHandler::execute_source_stream` replaces the six `df.execute_stream()` sites (CTAS streaming and replace, INSERT source, MERGE CoW rewrite, both MoR MERGE passes): plan, run `SortMemoryRule` with `max_fan_in = target_partitions`, refuse as `ResourceExhausted`, else `execute_stream`. `sqe_planner::sort_grant_bytes` is the one grant formula (read path uses it too). Also measured: with the rule's default fan-in of 8 and the 8 MiB grant floor the gate could never fire (2 MiB headroom holds 32 buffers), which is why the read-path rule has been silent; sizing fan-in from `target_partitions` makes it a real gate, on the write path only so far. Unit test: 64-way merge under an 8 MiB grant refused, 2-way admitted and streams. Left on #412: N partition merges for partitioned targets (the fanout writers are bounded by default since 905, the sort merge is not partition-aware), and whether the read path should size its fan-in from `target_partitions` as well. Verified: clippy with tests on planner, write, coordinator, server; sqe-write `write_handler` suite on the sqlite rig (116); planner sort tests (13); release-shape binary check.

> **STATUS 2026-09-11 (d): MCP catalog scoping (`feat/mcp-catalog-scoping`, stacked on !991); second of three MCP MRs.**
>
> `[mcp] write_catalogs` gates the change tool on the statement's
> parser-visible target: table of INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE/CREATE TABLE
> [AS]/ALTER TABLE/DROP/TRUNCATE/COMMENT, view of CREATE VIEW, schema of
> CREATE/DROP SCHEMA (`write_targets` in sqe-mcp, over the sqlparser 0.62
> AST). Sources stay reads. While gated, unresolvable targets and target-less
> kinds (CALL, grants, policy DDL) are refused, not guessed. `[[hive.roots]]`
> catalogs need the explicit entry even with an empty list, because Hive has
> no per-user storage credentials (reversal 1 of the Hive design): an agent
> writing there acts as the engine. The coordinator passes the root names
> (`mcp_server::hive_root_names`); ATTACHed Hive catalogs cannot exist over
> MCP since a session lives one request. `default_catalog`/`default_schema`
> per endpoint and per static principal are set on the session, so they drive
> `information_schema` qualification, name resolution and the gate alike. MCP
> classifies through `SqlFrontDoor::new(WireDialect::Mcp)` (pre-parse pipeline
> first, ORIGINAL text to the executor; the door's output is classification
> input with time-travel clauses stripped). Found: `SHOW STATS` resolves
> through `show_catalog` + `load_table`, so `get_table_stats` errors on a Hive
> table; documented, tool description says so. 29 sqe-mcp tests (7 new), 3
> sqe-core config tests. **NEXT (MR 3, `test/mcp-access-control-e2e`):**
> `tests/it` over `/mcp` against Polaris + Ranger for masks, row filters,
> denied tables invisible in `list_tables`, Hive write refusal; then
> `search_tables` and `sample_rows` tools. Later: Hive `SHOW STATS`.

> **STATUS 2026-09-11 (c): MCP calls are attributable in the audit trail (`feat/mcp-audit-identity`); first of three MCP MRs (audit identity -> catalog scoping -> access-control e2e).**
>
> Before this, an OCSF event for an AI agent's query through `/mcp` was
> indistinguishable from a Flight SQL client's: the Trino door stamps
> `Session.source` from `X-Trino-Source`, MCP never set it, and the audit
> `Actor` had no field for it anyway. Now `Actor.app_name` (OCSF
> `actor.app_name`) carries the session source for every protocol, and every
> MCP tool call stamps `mcp:<tool>:<client>/<version>`. The client comes from
> the `clientInfo` on `initialize`; SQE answers with an `Mcp-Session-Id` and
> applies the label to later calls that echo it under the same identity. The
> id is advisory (unknown, pre-restart, or another identity's id falls back to
> the sanitized `User-Agent`, then `mcp:<tool>`), and the registry is bounded
> (4096 entries, 24 h idle). The MCP auth rate limit now spends the same
> `(peer IP, bearer fingerprint)` tuple as the Trino path instead of
> `(peer IP, "mcp-bearer")`, which was one bucket per proxy address.
> `sqe_trino_compat::server::bearer_fingerprint` is public for that; one
> definition of "the same credential". 22 sqe-mcp tests (5 new), 71
> sqe-metrics audit tests, `quickstart/mcp/test-mcp.sh` gains two checks
> (not run here; needs the Docker stack). **NEXT (MR 2, `feat/mcp-catalog-scoping`):**
> `[mcp] write_catalogs` allowlist (Hive roots write with engine credentials,
> so the change tool needs a target gate), per-principal default catalog and
> schema, `WireDialect::Mcp` through `SqlFrontDoor`, Hive over MCP documented
> and `get_table_stats` checked against a Hive root. **MR 3:** `tests/it`
> cases over `/mcp` against Polaris + Ranger for masks, row filters, denied
> tables; then `search_tables` and `sample_rows` tools.

> **STATUS 2026-09-11 (b): worker heartbeat protocol tests get a 5 s wait bound (`fix/heartbeat-test-ci-margins`); the four-tests-at-once CI failure on !983 and !988 was the `2 * INTERVAL` first-hello bound.**
>
> `crates/sqe-worker/tests/protocol/heartbeat_roundtrip.rs` waited at most
> 200 ms (two 100 ms intervals) for the first hello and 100 to 300 ms for
> every later event. On a loaded aikido-test-rust runner all four rigs
> start together inside `cargo test --workspace --all-features` and the
> first hello, channel connect included, missed that bound twice (jobs
> 5333198, 5338280), failing all four tests in the same second; locally
> 5/5 green in 0.66 s. Every `wait_for` now uses one `BOUND` of 5 s.
> `wait_for` returns the moment its probe holds, so the healthy-run time is
> unchanged; only the failure mode moved from "slow runner" to "broken
> worker". Same class as the streaming idle and hive TTL margins of !985.
> The wider bound exposed a second, older race in `heartbeat_roundtrip`:
> the stub records the hello before its reply reaches the worker, and the
> test asserted the worker-side effects (`coordinator_accepted`, pinned
> ticket key) on the spot; 1 in 3 local runs failed there. Both are now
> polled with `wait_for`. 6/6 local runs of the protocol binary green.

> **STATUS 2026-09-10 (f): `SqliteRsCatalog` serialises every call on its one connection (`fix/sqlite-rs-serialise-connection`); fixes the "cannot start a transaction within a transaction" seen on !985.**
>
> The facade's worker serialises statements, not transactions. Two
> concurrent `update_table` commits on the shared `Connection` interleaved
> their `BEGIN`s (coordinator `maintenance_lease_test::concurrent_try_acquire_for_the_same_job_key_exactly_one_wins`
> under `--all-features`, where `sql-sqlite-rs` wins the JDBC `sqlite:` arm),
> and any autocommit statement from a third task during someone's
> transaction would have run inside it. `SharedConn { conn, gate: Mutex }`
> replaces the bare `Arc<Connection>`; `SharedConn::run` holds the gate for
> the whole blocking closure, so every catalog call, single statement or
> transaction, is exclusive. Regression test
> `concurrent_commits_and_writes_on_one_connection_never_nest_transactions`
> (8 concurrent property commits + 8 namespace creations on one connection,
> multi-thread runtime; outcomes must be success or the retryable conflict,
> end state consistent, oracle `integrity_check` ok). Verified: sqlite-rs
> tests 6/6 x3 and the coordinator lease tests 9/9 x3 on sqlite-rs
> (`--features test-sqlite,sqe-catalog/sql-sqlite-rs`), clippy clean.

> no `[licenses]` section rejects every license as "not explicitly allowed":
> `licenses FAILED` with about 600 rejections on every job this week (!977
> 622, !980 617, !982 605, !983 620), including `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. Pure
> noise that would also have hidden a real finding. The allow list is the

> what the blocking SBOM job enforces) with the sixteen expressions the
> graph carries (`cargo deny --all-features list -l license`); `r-efi`'s
> `MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR LGPL-2.1-or-later` passes on its MIT branch.
> Local `cargo deny --all-features check licenses`: `licenses ok`. Also
> today: three CI-timing flakes fixed (!984 config env lock, !985 hive TTL
> reload + streaming idle-timeout margins), none related to the sqlite-rs
> or ring work.

>
> `sql-sqlite-rs` replaces `sql-sqlite` in sqe-catalog's default set and is
> unconditional on sqe-cli's sqe-catalog dependency (`sqlite-c` = the sqlx
> + C engine as an opt-in fallback). It is deliberately not a sqe-cli
> default feature: the first version gated it on one and the release job,
> which builds with `--no-default-features` like the Dockerfile and the
> Makefile, hit the `compile_error!` (pipeline 1611344). `sqlite:` URLs on
> the JDBC paths go to `SqliteRsCatalog` too: `TYPE jdbc` in `mount.rs`
> (`build_jdbc` splits into a sqlite-rs branch and `build_jdbc_sqlx`) and
> `CatalogBackend::Jdbc` in `build_backend_catalog` (guarded arm, same
> `sqe-jdbc-session` scope as before). The `test-sqlite` features of
> sqe-coordinator and sqe-write drop `sql-postgres`, since the write rig's
> sqlite URL no longer needs sqlx. workspace-hack loses its hand pins on
> `sqlx` (`sqlite`) and `libsqlite3-sys` (`bundled`) and the loader pin
> goes to `rest` only; hakari no drift. `cargo tree -i libsqlite3-sys` and
> `-i sqlx` match nothing for sqe-cli, sqe-coordinator, sqe-worker.
> `maintenance.rs`'s race seam is now `cfg(all(test, feature =
> "test-sqlite"))`, matching its only user, so `clippy --all-targets -D
> warnings` is clean without the feature. Verified: mount_sqlite 5/5 and
> sqlite_rs 4/4 on defaults, sqe-cli embedded 54/54 under `sqlite-rs` and
> under `sqlite-c`, sqe-write `test-sqlite` write_handler 115/115 on
> sqlite-rs, coordinator `test-sqlite` maintenance 36/36, clippy clean on
> catalog/cli/write/coordinator/worker defaults and on sqe-catalog
> `--all-features`. **NEXT:** Lab271 reply with the four defects (see (b));
> re-vendor when #705 merges; delete `replace_row` when UPDATE binding is

> **STATUS 2026-09-10 (b): the embedded SQLite catalog runs on pure-Rust sqlite-rs behind `sql-sqlite-rs` (`feat/sqlite-rs-catalog`); sqlx stays the fallback.**
>
> `vendor/sqlite-rs/` is a library-only snapshot of Lab271's facade branch
> (`feat/013-api-facade` @ `e788241`, PR #705; zero runtime deps, `src/bin`
> dropped, SQE's own Cargo.toml). Vendored rather than a git dependency
> because cargo fetches optional git deps at resolve time and the repo is
> private to the runners. `sqe_catalog::sqlite_rs_catalog::SqliteRsCatalog`
> implements `iceberg::Catalog` over `sqlite_rs::api::Connection`, same
> two-table schema and statements as the vendored sqlx catalog, every call
> on `spawn_blocking`. Rules from Lab271's measured in-process data loss:
> one `Connection` per canonical path process-wide (`Weak` registry, no
> pool), no `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (#697; `table_names()` probe),
> named `UNIQUE` indexes instead of a declared composite PK on fresh files
> (#687), and `Busy` -> retryable. Wired into `TYPE sqlite` (`mount.rs`,
> preferred when compiled in) and sqe-cli `--embedded` (`sqlite-rs`
> feature, default-off). Running the catalog's real statements through
> the facade found **four engine defects**, each pinned by a test and
> worked around in the module (docs header lists them): (1) an `UPDATE`
> binds only its first parameter, so every update is DELETE + INSERT under
> `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`replace_row`, also the commit CAS); (2) a
> covering-index SELECT on an autoindexed table miscounts parameters
> (`list_tables` projects an unread column); (3) `rowid` is not
> selectable; (4) `sqlite_master` is not queryable. Verified: 7 catalog
> tests (fresh file, reopen, CAS, one-connection, **sqlx-written file
> adopted, written, read back by sqlx**, stock `sqlite3` integrity ok
> throughout), sqe-cli embedded 54/54 under both engines, clippy clean
> both configs, hakari no drift; end-to-end `sqe-cli --embedded` built
> with `--features sqlite-rs` creates a warehouse and adopts a sqlx one.
> Also shipped: `chore/drop-rusqlite` (!980). **NEXT:** send Lab271 the
> four defects plus the sample `sqe.db` files; flip `sqlite-rs` on by
> default once #705 merges and the defects close (re-vendor per
> `vendor/sqlite-rs/README.md`); then drop `sql-sqlite` from sqe-cli's
> default set so the C library leaves the embedded binary.
> **STATUS 2026-09-10 (d): ring is the only rustls crypto provider; aws-lc-sys (the BoringSSL C build) is out of every graph (`build/rustls-ring-provider`).**
>
> Both providers were compiled and aws-lc was the one in use. Three roots:
> the workspace `reqwest` 0.13 `rustls` feature (= `__rustls-aws-lc-rs`),
> `rustls` declared with default features in sqe-core and sqe-coordinator
> (`aws_lc_rs` + `prefer-post-quantum`), and `aws-config` with its default
> `default-https-client`, which aws-smithy-runtime hardcodes to
> `aws-smithy-http-client/rustls-aws-lc`; that came in through the vendored
> REST crate's `aws-sigv4` feature and sqe-catalog's glue/s3tables. Fixes:
> reqwest `rustls-no-provider`; rustls `default-features = false` +
> `["ring","std","tls12","logging"]`; aws-config `default-features = false`
> everywhere with an explicit ring `aws_smithy_http_client` HTTPS client
> (rest `sigv4.rs`, sqe-catalog `aws_config.rs`, same shape the vendored
> Glue/S3 Tables crates already used, #133). hakari regenerated; the
> workspace-hack pins on aws-config, aws-smithy-http-client and
> rustls-webpki `aws-lc-rs` fell away. `cargo tree -i aws-lc-sys` matches
> nothing, default and `--all-features`. **The trap:** reqwest 0.13 under
> `rustls-no-provider` panics at `Client` construction unless a process
> default provider is installed (it does not auto-select from crate
> features), and clients are built in 25 places across a dozen crates plus
> tests. So sqe-core installs ring pre-main (`#[ctor::ctor]` in `tls.rs`,
> new dep `ctor 0.5`), the five binaries call `ensure_crypto_provider()`
> first thing, and the three client crates that do not link sqe-core
> (sqe-trino-protocol, sqe-quack-client, sqe-bench) install it themselves.
> Verified with the real binary: `sqe-cli --protocol http --tls` to a valid
> host reaches HTTP 422 (TLS fine), to self-signed.badssl.com fails on the
> certificate (verification live); sqe-auth 282, trino-protocol 19,
> quack-client/server, sqe-core tls 30, coordinator tls 5 all pass; clippy
> `-D warnings` clean on the eight touched crates. Trade-off accepted: no
> FIPS module and no post-quantum hybrid key exchange until ring or a
> pure-Rust provider (graviola) offers them; same BoringSSL lineage
> otherwise. **NEXT:** measure the cold-build win in CI (aws-lc-sys was the
> single slowest C build); evaluate `rustls-graviola` as a drop-in once
> ring is the sole provider.

> **STATUS 2026-09-09 (b): finding 707 (#627) closed on `fix/flight-use-session-scope-707`: `USE` over Flight SQL scopes the session, and the bench Flight client stops qualifying table names.**
>
> `USE [catalog.]schema` was a logged no-op in the engine; only the Trino
> layer honoured it, by handing the scope back as `X-Trino-Set-*` headers the
> client echoes. Flight SQL has no such channel, so the bench's Flight client
> still rewrote every query with the tokenizer qualifier. Now the handler
> mutates the stored session the same way `SET WRITE_BRANCH` does
> (`SessionManager::set_default_scope`, copy-and-reinsert), so every later
> request on the same bearer token, on any connection, resolves unqualified
> names in the new scope; `USE schema` keeps the catalog, a three-part target
> is a syntax error. `current_schema()` reads the same field, so it is the
> observable. The first test of the new setter hung: `set_write_branch`
> held the DashMap read guard while inserting into the same shard, a
> deadlock that `SET WRITE_BRANCH` over Flight had in production and no
> test exercised; both setters now go through `update_session`, which
> clones the entry and drops the guard before the insert. `FlightSqlBenchClient::with_schema` sends one `USE` lazily on
> the first query (`tokio::sync::OnceCell`) and reports `default_schema()`,
> which switches `QualifyTables::Auto` off; the `run` verb builds one scoped
> client per suite (`[golden.]<ns>`), `create_client` applies
> `[catalog.]schema` for `test`/`run`. A tokenless Flight client keeps
> qualifying (no session to scope). Tests: `session_manager` unit,
> `parse_use_target` unit, `flight_sql_smoke::use_over_flight_scopes_later_statements_on_the_same_token`
> (fresh channel, same token; catalog.schema form; bad target; other session
> untouched). Docs: sql-reference/dml.md session-control rows,
> benchmarks/queries/README.md. Not re-run: `sqe-bench compare tpch` 22/22
> parity on a live stack (needs the docker Trino rig).
> **NEXT:** #659 partials (508 and 108 are deletions), 807 item 3 (docker
> runner), the #4xx duplicate close (109 issues; the assistant's tooling is
> not allowed to close issues, so this is a `glab api` loop run by hand).
> **STATUS 2026-09-09 (c): CI test job anatomy and the two cheap fixes, on `perf/ci-test-phase`.**
>
> Pipeline 1609830's `aikido-test-rust` was 21m: 12m41 `cargo test --no-run`
> (623 crates, cold: the cache key is per branch and holds only the crate
> registry; `target/` is 33 GB against a 5 GiB cache limit; sccache is off
> until the bucket exists on the prd endpoint), 2m42 test execution, 5m06
> clippy serial after the suite, ~30 s the rest. Three levers, in payoff

> **STATUS 2026-09-09: Hive table-load round trips (#663) on `perf/hive-table-load-recheck`: one metadata round trip per recheck window instead of a manifest HEAD plus up to two data-prefix listings per lookup.**
>
> Tracker housekeeping first: the 2026-09-04 review findings were filed
> twice (#433–#541 and #543–#651). Every speed finding I had queued (#463
> 307, #464 308, #477 407, #494 512, #511 701, #478 408) is the stale copy
> of a finding !952 closed under its #5xx number; the #4xx series is 109
> open duplicates. The only speed work left with a Hive angle was not
> filed: `HiveSchemaProvider::table` runs per table reference, per
> `information_schema` row and for the tag source, and each call paid a
> HEAD (manifest ETag), for CSV/JSON a LIST to read the codec off a key
> (the doc said "per cache miss"; the code ran it every call), and with
> neither ANALYZE stats nor an index a full LIST for size statistics.
> Fix: `HiveMetadataCache` recheck window (`[hive] metadata_recheck_secs`,
> default 10, `0` = always check) mirrors `catalog.authorization_recheck_secs`;
> `HiveTableFacts` (codec, listed bytes) live on the manifest's cache entry
> and expire with it or on the TTL; a hit marks the entry verified without
> resetting `loaded_at`, which fixes a second defect: ETag-less sources
> (Glue) had their TTL extended on every hit, so a continuously queried
> table never reloaded. All DDL/write paths already `invalidate`, so
> same-process writes stay immediately visible; the window only delays
> another writer (book page updated). Tests: counting source + counting
> store in `hive_provider_test.rs` (1 HEAD / 1 GET / 2 LIST for three
> lookups; recheck 0; invalidate; TTL lapse). Stacked on !974.
> **NEXT:** close the #4xx duplicates (109 issues, needs a go-ahead), then
> the Flight `USE` / default-schema gap (#627), #659 partials, the
> docker-capable CI runner (807 item 3).

> **STATUS 2026-09-08 (late): build-time C4 for `sqe-catalog` on `perf/catalog-async-hubs`: trait solving 39.5 s -> 7.2 s; cold chain 108.8 s (main) -> 84.9 s.**
>
> Re-measured main first (`touch sqe-core`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`): 108.8 s,
> down from 173 s at the start of the program (!961 + !963). Then profiled the
> catalog and aggregated `evaluate_obligation` by *predicate*: 39.5 s of
> `<datafusion_expr::... as Send/Sync>` proofs, each solved once per async
> method because every method has its own parameter environment. The cost is
> per coroutine that captures an `Expr`-bearing value, and `async_trait` makes
> every trait `async fn` such a coroutine. Three shapes fix it without touching
> DataFusion's traits: pass-through wrappers written in `async_trait`'s
> desugared form (no coroutine: `ReservedMemTable::scan`, the Hive DML hooks),
> hubs boxed at the definition (`SessionCatalog::load_table`,
> `SqeTableProvider::scan_impl`, `build_columns_batch`, `plan_view`), and
> erasing what a block holds across an await (`MemTable` carries `Expr` in its
> column defaults, so `Arc<dyn TableProvider>` first; `ListingOptions` carries
> `Expr`, so the Hive `table()` lists files before it builds the
> `ListingTable`). The residue (`HiveTableProvider::scan`/`scan_with_args`
> must own the rewritten `Vec<Expr>` the inner scan borrows; `ScopedTable::scan`
> holds a `SessionState`) is a fourth shape: `send_erased::SendErased<T>`
> wraps the value in a type whose `Send + Sync` is stated once (unsafe impls
> for `Vec<Expr>`, `&[Expr]`, `SessionState`, each checked structurally by a
> unit test), so the coroutine proof never descends into DataFusion. That
> took obligations 13.9 s -> 7.2 s and the chain 93.8 s -> 84.9 s. Plan:
> docs/internal/plans/2026-09-07-compile-time-async-hubs.md, "second look".
> Gates: sqe-catalog all test binaries 846, `it` 150, live Hive e2e 5, clippy
> clean. Behaviour
> unchanged; the metadata TVF scan stops forwarding `filters` to its
> `MemTable`, which never received any (default pushdown is unsupported).
> **NEXT:** the Flight `USE` / default-schema gap (closes #627), #659 partials,
> the docker-capable CI runner (807 item 3). Parked, not in this repo: A2
> sccache bucket on object02 prd (lifecycle rule + RO key, then the five CI
> variables); C3 runner with a persistent target dir (platform team).
> **STATUS 2026-09-08 (night): finding 610 (#617) on `fix/lineage-masked-and-untraced-610`: column lineage reports masked source columns as MASKED and says when a trace is incomplete.**
>
> `PolicySummary` gains `masked_fields: Vec<(table, column)>`, the scan
> `TableReference` display plus column for every masked or restricted column
> (a restricted column holds NULL, so it counts as masked); `summarise_policies`
> fills it and `merge` folds it. The coordinator passes it into the lineage
> `QueryCompleteCtx` / `QueryFailCtx` (new `masked_fields`), and the new
> `extract::extract_lineage_governed` rewrites every column-lineage dependency
> on one of those scan columns to `{DIRECT, MASKED, masking: true}`. The
> decision comes from the enforcer's own record rather than from guessing at
> projection shapes, and `sqe-lineage` stays free of a policy-crate dependency
> (plain string tuples cross the boundary). Completeness: `trace_plan_reporting`
> collects the plan node kinds the tracer has no rule for (`Unnest`,
> `RecursiveQuery`, `Values`, multi-input extensions, ...) through a
> thread-local active only during a reporting walk; when non-empty the run
> carries the custom facet `sqe_lineageQuality: {complete: false, untraced:
> [...]}`. The old `extract_lineage` entry point and every existing extract
> test are unchanged. Book: operations/openlineage.md, new section "Policy and
> completeness in column lineage". Tests: `extract_governed_test.rs` (masked vs
> identity, namespace-only table key, `Unnest` facet, plain entry point
> unchanged), summary test extended. Gates: sqe-lineage all binaries green,
> sqe-policy 282, coordinator streaming tests, clippy clean. Not done from the
> proposal: the `sqe_lineage_incomplete_total` counter (the emitter has no
> metrics registry handle beyond the dropped counter; add when a lineage
> metrics block exists).

> **STATUS 2026-09-08 (evening): finding 205 (#558) on `fix/show-stats-restricted-columns-205`: `SHOW STATS` is governed by the same effective policy as a scan.**
>
> `handle_show_stats` read the Iceberg schema directly and listed every
> column, and reported the snapshot's `total-records` regardless of the
> caller's row filter; `information_schema.columns` (and so `SHOW COLUMNS`,

> on the rule; `tests/it/show_stats_policy_e2e.rs` (`needs:polaris`) runs the
> same table through a passthrough handler and a handler wired to an
> `InMemoryPolicyStore` (new `common::setup_handler_with_policy_store`) and
> checks the column list, the NULL count and the filtered `count(*)`. Gates:

> **STATUS 2026-09-08 (afternoon): finding 806 (#638), file table functions, on `fix/file-tvf-prepare-806`: `read_parquet` / `read_csv` / `read_json` infer their schema on the async path; `call` only looks the provider up.**
>
> `TableFunctionImpl::call` is synchronous and DataFusion invokes it while
> planning, so the three file readers bridged onto object storage from
> inside the planner (`runtime_bridge::block_on_compat`, five sites). New
> module `crates/sqe-catalog/src/file_tvf_prepare.rs`: each reader
> implements `PreparableTableFunction::prepare(exprs)` (parse, path policy,
> build the `ListingTable`, store it in the function's `PreparedProviders`
> keyed by the literal arguments) and its `call` re-runs the policy and
> looks the provider up. A call that was never prepared is a planning error
> that names the function and `prepare_statement`, not a fallback: that is
> how a new planner entry point that forgets the step gets noticed (it
> caught the CTAS path on the first live run). `FileTableFunctions::register`
> replaces `ctx.register_udtf` for the readers and records them in a
> `SessionConfig` extension; `prepare_statement(&ctx, sql)` parses the final
> SQL, converts the file-function arguments (`'k=v'` literals, `k => v`,
> `k = v`, numbers, booleans) to the `Expr`s the planner will pass, and
> awaits each `prepare`. Hooked before every `ctx.sql` in the coordinator
> (execute, the Flight stream planner, `get_schema`, parameter typing, the
> three EXPLAIN paths, the Hive CTAS schema probe) plus once in the
> statement dispatcher, gated on the text naming a file function, which
> covers the write handler's many derived sub-statements (CTAS / INSERT ...
> SELECT / MERGE) through the session-cached context. The embedded CLI
> prepares before its `ctx.sql`. `read_delta.rs` keeps its bridge: the module
> is unwired pending a DataFusion-55 delta-rs. Gates: catalog unit 637 (six
> new for the module, the `read_json` tests now plan through `prepare`),
> `it` 150, the three `test_read_*_local_file` CTAS cases against the live
> stack, sqe-cli 109, clippy clean.
>
> Left of 806 after this: `SqeCatalogProvider::schema()`'s namespace re-list
> on miss (#368) and `WritableIcebergCatalog`'s create/drop-namespace hooks
> (three sites); both already report the bridge error as an error, and the
> coordinator routes CREATE/DROP SCHEMA through `catalog_ops` asynchronously,
> so the remaining consumer is the embedded CLI's `ctx.sql("CREATE SCHEMA")`.
> **NEXT:** those three sites (then #638 closes), 205/212 (#653), 610 (#657),
> 627, #659 partials; the docker-capable CI runner for the `polaris` group.

> **STATUS 2026-09-08: finding 806 (#638), Hive half, on `fix/hive-listing-snapshots-806`: the Hive catalog's synchronous listing hooks serve snapshots; no `block_on` bridge left in the Hive read path.**
>
> `HiveCatalogProvider::schema_names()` and `HiveSchemaProvider::table_names()`
> used to list the metastore through `runtime_bridge::block_on_compat` inside

> now serve `HiveListings` snapshots (`crates/sqe-catalog/src/hive/provider.rs`),
> per provider, reusing the Iceberg `NamespaceListing` shape: last known
> names plus `stale_error`/`denied`, so an outage is served as the previous
> list with a warning and `table_exist` answers unknown as present rather
> than absent. `refresh_referenced(sql, default_catalog, default_schema)`
> fills them on the async path: the database list when older than the TTL,
> the tables of every database the statement references in that root
> (`cat.db.t`; `db.t` and the session schema when the root is the default
> catalog), and every database when the statement reads
> `information_schema`. The coordinator calls it from one new helper,
> `QueryHandler::refresh_listing_snapshots`, which also carries the Iceberg
> refresh and now runs on every path that plans through `ctx.sql`: execute,
> the Flight stream planner (`plan_sql_for_stream`, which had no Iceberg
> refresh before), `get_schema` and `columns_for_table` (DESCRIBE / SHOW
> COLUMNS). The embedded CLI refreshes its attached roots before `ctx.sql`.
> DDL invalidation drops the table and the database snapshot. Six unit tests
> pin the rules with a scripted metadata source. Gates: hive_ddl_test 20,
> hive_mount_test 8, sqe-cli 109, `it` 150, clippy clean.
>

> **STATUS 2026-09-07 (evening): coordinator crate split started; MR A (write-path decoupling, no file moves) on `refactor/write-path-decouple`.**
>
> The compile-time measurement that decides the shape is in
> `docs/internal/plans/2026-09-07-coordinator-crate-split.md`: after a touch
> of sqe-core the whole first-party chain rebuilds in 154 s, of which the
> coordinator library alone is 96 s (64 s of it type-checking) and its
> binary 30 s more; every other crate is done by 44 s. Untangling the
> policy/catalog and worker/coordinator edges (plan item C1) could move the
> coordinator's start by at most 12 s, so it is dropped in favour of C2. A
> chain of crates does not shorten a cold build; only sibling crates do, and
> the coordinator's one big sibling pair is the write path and the
> distribution path. MR A cuts every edge from the write path into the rest
> of the crate without moving a file: the five identifier helpers move from
> `catalog_ops` to a new `ident` module, `commit_with_retry` and
> `is_conflict_message` to a new `commit` module, `collect_live_data_files`
> from `maintenance` to `writer`, the two live-delete helpers are imported
> from `sqe_compaction` directly, and the two `session_context` calls
> (Polaris discovery, session-cache invalidation) go through a
> `CatalogDiscovery` trait that `QueryHandler::new` installs as
> `PolarisCatalogDiscovery`. Behaviour is unchanged when the trait is
> installed; a `WriteHandler` without it (unit tests) treats a non-default
> 3-part target as an unknown catalog and skips session-cache invalidation.
> The compiler cache is switched off for now: the acc StorageGRID endpoint

> **STATUS 2026-09-04: Puffin NDV statistics consumer implemented behind two flags on `feat/puffin-ndv-statistics`; registry arm measured negative at SF1.**
>
> The DataFusion 55 handover left Phase 6 (statistics adoption) as the next action. The worktree it described as empty held the consumer half uncommitted under `cargo fmt` noise; the real changes are now committed. `PuffinNdvCache` reads the `ndv` blob property straight from the snapshot `StatisticsFile` metadata (no sidecar fetch), `SqeTableProvider::scan_with_args` answers `StatisticsRequest::DistinctCount` as inexact column statistics, and `PuffinStatisticsRequestRule` asks for them on every projected scan column. `query.puffin_statistics` (env `SQE_QUERY__PUFFIN_STATISTICS`) turns that on; a separate `query.statistics_registry` flips `datafusion.optimizer.use_statistics_registry`. Both default false.
>
> Two bugs surfaced by checking the warehouse instead of the logs. A full bench load produced zero `.stats` files: the coordinator routes CTAS and INSERT through the streaming handlers, and only the buffered handlers called the sidecar emitter, so the Phase F writer had never run on the default path. `ThetaSketchAccumulator` now taps the batch stream. Then the first sidecars showed `ndv = 0` on Decimal128 and Date32 (admitted as sketchable, no handler, one WARN per batch) and no blob at all for strings, because DataFusion 55 reads Parquet strings as `Utf8View`. All handled; a unit test asserts every admitted type yields an estimate. Orders at SF0.1 now reads o_orderkey 148715 (true 150000), o_clerk 1000 (exact).
>
> Evidence in `docs/evidence/perf/puffin-ndv-ab-2026-09-04.md`. SF0.1 Trino compare with everything on: TPC-H 22/22, SSB 11/13 (known vacuous pair). SF1 plans: the NDV-only arm is identical to baseline except q17; the registry arm flips the hash build onto lineitem (or its aggregate) in q03/q05/q17/q18/q21 and reverses the runtime-filter direction, which is the opposite of what `dim_build_swap` and `star_schema_reorder` do on purpose. Counterbalanced timing: q17 1.40x slower in both rounds, q03/q05 1.23x; the NDV-only arm is runtime-neutral within this box's 20-40% noise. Verdict: registry stays off, NDV requests are safe but earn nothing yet, `write.puffin.stats` default stays off until a consumer pays for the write.
>
> **NEXT:** repeat `on` vs `off` on the clean rig to confirm the q03/q05/q17 direction; then decide between teaching the SQE join rules to read `distinct_count` and registering an SQE `StatisticsProvider` ahead of DataFusion's built-in join provider. Also open: peak memory was not captured (harness reports wall time only), and the branch commits are unsigned (signing key was not in the agent).
>
